such that
h(g(x))=0
, therefore
x
is integral over
\widetilde{S}
hence it lies in
\tilde{B}
, hence
\overline{x}
lies in
\tilde{B}/\mathfrak{p}\tilde{B}
.
Thus we have shown that
S
is henselian.

To conclude the proof of the implication
\Rightarrow
, it is enough to further check that
(k,v)
is strictly henselian in case
of the v-étale-topology and tamely closed in case of the tame topology, where
k=S/\mathfrak{p}S
and
v
is the valuation associated to
\widetilde{S}/\mathfrak{p}
. The former case holds since
\widetilde{S}
is strictly henselian. To show the latter case, take a finite extension
k^{\prime}/k
and a valuation
v^{\prime}
on
k^{\prime}
over
v
such that
v^{\prime}/v
is tame.
We want to prove
k=k^{\prime}
.
Since it is separable, there exists
\overline{\omega}\in\mathcal{O}_{v^{\prime}}
such that
k^{\prime}=k[\overline{\omega}]
and
\mathcal{O}_{v^{\prime}}
is the integral closure of
\mathcal{O}_{v}[\overline{\omega}]
.
Let
\overline{p}\in\mathcal{O}_{v}[T]
be the monic minimal polynomial of
\overline{\omega}
over
k
.
Since
S
is henselian and
\widetilde{S}=S\times_{k}\mathcal{O}_{v}
, there is
p\in\widetilde{S}[T]
that maps to
\overline{p}
in
k[T]
giving a finite étale extension
S\hookrightarrow S^{\prime}=S[T]/(p)
, with
S^{\prime}
henselian local with residue field
k^{\prime}=S^{\prime}/\mathfrak{p}S^{\prime}
.
Let
\widetilde{S}^{\prime}:=S^{\prime}\times_{k^{\prime}}\mathcal{O}_{v^{\prime}}
.

Claim 5.5
.

\widetilde{S}^{\prime}
is the integral closure of
\widetilde{S}[T]/(p)
in
S^{\prime}
.

Indeed, let
R=\widetilde{S}[T]/(p)
. Note that
\widetilde{S}^{\prime}
is integrally closed in
S^{\prime}
by definition.
Furthermore, the image of
T
in
S^{\prime}
lies in
\widetilde{S}^{\prime}
since its image
\overline{\omega}
in
k^{\prime}=S^{\prime}/\mathfrak{p}S^{\prime}
lies in
\mathcal{O}_{v^{\prime}}
.
Thus the natural map
R\to S^{\prime}
factors via
\widetilde{S}^{\prime}
and it remains to show that
\widetilde{S}^{\prime}
is integral over
R
.
To this end we observe that
\mathfrak{p}S^{\prime}
lies in
R
and in
\widetilde{S}^{\prime}
and that the induced map on the quotients

R/\mathfrak{p}S^{\prime}\to\widetilde{S}^{\prime}/\mathfrak{p}S^{\prime}
is the integral extension
\mathcal{O}_{v}[\overline{\omega}]\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{v^{\prime}}
.
Hence
\widetilde{S}^{\prime}
is integral over
R
.

Recall
\mathcal{T}=(\operatorname{Spec}(S),\operatorname{Spec}(\widetilde{S}))=\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{\alpha}\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}
with

\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}=(\operatorname{Spec}(S_{\alpha}),\operatorname{Spec}(\widetilde{S}_{\alpha}))
.
We show
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}:=(\operatorname{Spec}(S^{\prime}),\operatorname{Spec}(\widetilde{S}^{\prime}))\in\widetilde{(X,\tilde{X})_{\tau}}
.
Since
\widetilde{S}=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}\widetilde{S}_{\alpha}
, there exists
\alpha_{0}\in A
and
p_{\alpha_{0}}\in\widetilde{S}_{\alpha_{0}}[T]
mapping to
p
. Letting
p_{\alpha}
be the image of
p_{\alpha_{0}}
in
\widetilde{S}_{\alpha}[T]
, we have
S^{\prime}=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{\alpha\geq\alpha_{0}}S_{\alpha}^{\prime}
with
S_{\alpha}^{\prime}=S_{\alpha}[T]/(p_{\alpha})
.
By construction,
S_{\alpha}^{\prime}=S^{\prime}_{\alpha_{0}}\otimes_{S_{\alpha_{0}}}S_{\alpha}
.
By
[
Sta16
,
Tag 01SR
]
,
S_{\alpha}\to S_{\alpha}^{\prime}
is étale for
\alpha\gg\alpha_{0}
,
so
\operatorname{Spec}(\widetilde{S}_{\alpha}^{\prime})
is étale over
X
since
\operatorname{Spec}(S_{\alpha})
is étale over
X
.
Let
\widetilde{S}_{\alpha}^{\prime}
be the integral closure of
\widetilde{S}_{\alpha}[T]/(p_{\alpha})
in
S_{\alpha}^{\prime}
.
Since
\operatorname{Spec}(\widetilde{S}_{\alpha})
is ift over
\tilde{X}
,
\operatorname{Spec}(\widetilde{S}_{\alpha}^{\prime})
is ift over
\tilde{X}
.
By Claim
5.5
, we have

\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{\alpha}\widetilde{S}^{\prime}_{\alpha}=\widetilde{S}^{\prime}
noting that taking integral closures commutes with filtered colimits.
By construction,
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}^{\prime}:=(\operatorname{Spec}(S_{\alpha}^{\prime}),\operatorname{Spec}(\widetilde{S}_{\alpha}^{\prime}))\in(X,\tilde{X})_{\tau}
and we have

\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{\alpha}\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
5.4
,
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}
is a tame covering for
\alpha\gg\alpha_{0}
. Since
\mathcal{T}
is tame acyclic, this implies that for
\alpha\gg\alpha_{0}
, the map
S_{\alpha}\to S
factors through
S_{\alpha}^{\prime}
, which implies
k=k^{\prime}
as desired.

\Leftarrow
Take
\mathcal{T}=(\operatorname{Spec}(S),\operatorname{Spec}(\widetilde{S}))
with
\widetilde{S}=S\times_{k}\mathcal{O}_{v}
as in Proposition
5.2
.
We want to show that for any covering
h:\mathcal{V}=(V,\tilde{V})\to\mathcal{U}=(U,\tilde{U})
in
(X,\tilde{X})_{\gamma}
with
\gamma={\operatorname{v\acute{e}t}}
or
\gamma=t
,

(5.5.1)

\operatorname{Hom}_{\widetilde{(X,\tilde{X})_{\tau}}}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{V})\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\widetilde{(X,\tilde{X})_{\tau}}}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{U})

is surjective.
Clearly, it suffices to consider the generator coverings so we may assume that
h
is either a quasi-modification or strict étale covering in case
\gamma={\operatorname{v\acute{e}t}}
and
a tame covering in case
\gamma=t
.
Write
T=\operatorname{Spec}(S)
,
\tilde{T}=\operatorname{Spec}(\widetilde{S})
and take
(f,\tilde{f}):(T,\tilde{T})\to(U,\tilde{U})
.

If
h
is a strict étale covering so that
\tilde{V}\to\tilde{U}
is an étale covering and

V=\tilde{V}\times_{\tilde{U}}U
,
\tilde{f}
admits a lift
\tilde{g}:\tilde{T}\to\tilde{V}
since
\widetilde{S}
is strictly henselian. Moreover, the composite
T\to\tilde{T}\overset{\tilde{g}}{\longrightarrow}\tilde{V}
and
f:T\to U
induce

g:T\to V=\tilde{V}\times_{\tilde{U}}U
so that
(g,\tilde{g})
gives a lift of
(f,\tilde{f})
.

Assume that
h
is a quasi-modification.
Then,
f:T\to U
lifts to
g:T\to V
because
V\to U
is an isomorphism. By the valuative criterion for
universal closedness, the composite

\operatorname{Spec}(k)\hookrightarrow T\overset{g}{\longrightarrow}V\to\tilde{V}
extends to a morphism

q:\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{v})\to\tilde{V}
. These morphisms factor through some open affine of
\tilde{V}
, so
q
and
T\overset{g}{\longrightarrow}V\to\tilde{V}
glue to give a morphism

\tilde{g}:\tilde{T}=\operatorname{Spec}(\widetilde{S})\to\tilde{V}
since
\tilde{T}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{v}\times_{k}S)=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{v})\sqcup_{\operatorname{Spec}(k)}T
is the categorical pushout in the category of affine schemes. Thus, we get a lifting
(g,\tilde{g})
of
(f,\tilde{f})
.

Finally, assume that
h
is a tame covering and
(k,v)
is tamely closed.
Let
x\in U
be the image of
\operatorname{Spec}(k)\hookrightarrow T\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}U
and let
v_{x}
be the restriction of
v
to the residue field
\kappa(x)
. Note, as
f
is pro-étale, the field extension
k/\kappa(x)
is separable algebraic.
By assumption, there is a point
y\in V
and a valuation
v_{y}
on its residue field
\kappa(y)

extending
v_{x}
such that the extension
(\kappa(y),v_{y})/(\kappa(x),v_{x})
is tame. Since
(k,v)
is tamely closed,
there exists a map of valued fields
(\kappa(y),v_{y})\to(k,v)
which factors
(\kappa(x),v_{x})\to(k,v)
.
Since
V\to U
is an étale covering and
S
is henselian, this implies that

f:T=\operatorname{Spec}(S)\to U
admits a lift
g:T\to V
.
By the same argument as in the case of quasi-modifications,
g
extends to

\tilde{g}:\tilde{T}\to\tilde{V}
so that we get a lifting
(g,\tilde{g})
of
(f,\tilde{f})
. This completes the proof.

Remark 5.6
.

By Remark
2.5
(1)
and Deligne’s completeness theorem,
[
AGV72b
, Proposition VI.9.0]
or

[
Joh77
, Theorem 7.44, Corollary 7.17]
, the fiber functors
\varphi_{\mathcal{T}}
from (
5.1.2
) for
\mathcal{T}
satisfying the condition of Proposition
5.2
form a conservative family, i.e., a morphism
f
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X,\tilde{X})_{\gamma})
is an isomorphism if and only if
\varphi_{\mathcal{T}}(f)
is an isomorphism of sets for all
\gamma
-acyclic
\mathcal{T}
.
Equivalently, a morphism
\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{U}
in
(X,\tilde{X})_{\tau}
is a
\gamma
-covering if and only if

\operatorname{Hom}_{\widetilde{(X,\tilde{X})_{\tau}}}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{V})\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\widetilde{(X,\tilde{X})_{\tau}}}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{U})

is surjective for all such
\mathcal{T}
,
[
AGV72a
, Proposition IV.6.5(a)]
.

Remark 5.7
.

If the valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{v}
has finite height, then there exists
a\in\mathcal{O}_{v}
such that
k=\mathcal{O}_{v}[1/a]
(see
[
FK18
, Proposition 0.6.7.2 and Exercise 0.6.4]
). In this case, it is closer to the modulus pairs of
[
KM
]
.

Remark 5.8
.

Let
(\operatorname{Spec}(A),\operatorname{Spec}(A\times_{k}\mathcal{O}_{v}))
be
{\operatorname{v\acute{e}t}}
acyclic. Let
(\operatorname{Spec}(B),\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{B}))\to(\operatorname{Spec}(A),\operatorname{Spec}(A\times_{k}\mathcal{O}_{v}))
be a tame covering in the sense of Definition
2.2
(3)
. Since
A
is henselian local, we can refine it so that
B\to A
is finite étale asssociated to a finite separable extension of
k\hookrightarrow k^{\prime}
. By tameness, there exists a valuation
w
on
k^{\prime}
extending
v
such that
\tilde{B}\to B\to k^{\prime}
factors through
\mathcal{O}_{w}
. This implies that the map
\tilde{B}\to B
factors through
B\times_{k^{\prime}}\mathcal{O}_{w}
, therefore the covering
(\operatorname{Spec}(B),\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{B}))
is refined by
(\operatorname{Spec}(B),\operatorname{Spec}(B\times_{k}\mathcal{O}_{w}))
. Moreover, by Lemma
2.1
(1)
we can further refine it so that we have that
k^{\prime}/k
is Galois.

6.
Čech comparison

In this section, we prove a comparison theorem of tame cohomology with Čech cohomology (see Proposition
6.5
).

We fix a quasi-compact open immersion
X\to\tilde{X}
of qcqs schemes.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
\mathcal{U}=(U,\tilde{U})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
, let
x\in U
and let
U_{x}^{h}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{U,x}^{h})
be the henselization at
x
. An
x
-local object over
\mathcal{U}
is
\mathcal{T}:=(\operatorname{Spec}(B),\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{B}))\in\widetilde{(X,\tilde{X})_{t}}
with a map
\mathcal{T}\to\mathcal{U}
such that

(1)

B
is henselian local with residue field
k
,
\operatorname{Spec}(B)\to U
factors through
U_{x}^{h}
and the map
\mathcal{O}_{U,x}^{h}\to B
is local and ind-étale;

(2)

There is a valuation
v
on
k
such that
(k,v)
is tamely closed and
\operatorname{Spec}k\to U
restricts to

\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{v}\to\tilde{U}
;

(3)

\tilde{B}=B\times_{k}\mathcal{O}_{v}
.

Example 6.2
.

Let
\mathcal{U}=(U,\tilde{U})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
, let
x\in U
and
k(x)
be its residue field. Let
(x,v,\varepsilon_{v})\in\mathrm{Spa}(U,\tilde{U})
and choose an extension
\overline{v}
to a separable closure
\overline{k(x)}
of
k(x)
and
k(x)\hookrightarrow k(x)_{v}^{t}
be the tame closure of
k(x)
with respect to the valuation
v
. Let
\mathcal{O}_{U,x}^{h}\to\mathcal{O}_{U,x}^{t}
be the ind-étale map corresponding to the field extension
k(x)\hookrightarrow k(x)^{t}_{v}
and let
\mathcal{O}_{v}^{t}\subseteq k(x)^{t}_{v}
be the valuation ring of the restriction of
\overline{v}
to
k(x)^{t}_{v}
.
Then,
\mathcal{U}_{(x,v)}:=(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{U,x}^{t}),\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{U,x}^{t}\times_{k(x)_{v}^{t}}\mathcal{O}_{v}^{t}))
is an
x
-local object over
\mathcal{U}
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{U}_{(x,v)}\to\mathcal{U}
is a cofiltered limit of maps
\mathcal{U}_{i}\to\mathcal{U}
in
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
which is tame over
(x,v,\varepsilon_{v})
.

The following Lemma is a tame version of
[
Art71
, Theorem 3.4]
. The statement and the proof are close
to
[
HS20
, Corollary 11.7]
, which is a version for discretely ringed adic spaces.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
\mathcal{Y}=(Y,\tilde{Y})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
such that
\tilde{Y}
satisfies the property that every finite set of points is contained in an affine open. For
1\leq i\leq n
, let
x_{i}\in Y
and let
\mathcal{P}_{i}
be
x_{i}
-local objects over
\tilde{Y}
. Then
\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{P}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\ldots\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\mathcal{P}_{n}\in\widetilde{(X,\tilde{X})_{\tau}}
is affine and is a disjoint union of
x
-local objects, where either
x=x_{i}
for some
i
or
x
is a generization of
all
x_{i}
, i.e., the
x_{i}
lie in the closure of
x
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{P}_{i}=(\operatorname{Spec}(A_{i}),\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{A}_{i}))
with
\tilde{A}_{i}=A_{i}\times_{k_{i}}V_{i}
, where
k_{i}
is the residue field of
A_{i}
and
V_{i}

is a valuation field on
k_{i}
. Let
\operatorname{Spec}(V_{i})\to\tilde{Y}
be the induced map on the valuation rings, and let
\tilde{y}_{i}
be the respective images of the closed points. Let
\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{A})\subseteq\tilde{Y}
be an affine open containing
x_{i}
and
\tilde{y}_{i}
for all
i
.
As
\tilde{y}_{i}
is a specialization of
x_{i}
, we have natural maps

\tilde{A}\to\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{Y},\tilde{y}_{i}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{Y},x_{i}}\to A_{i}\to k_{i},

and by the definition of
\tilde{y}_{i}
its composition factors via
V_{i}\hookrightarrow k_{i}
.
Therefore all the maps
\operatorname{Spec}(V_{i})\to\tilde{Y}
above factor through
\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{A})
, therefore

\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{A}_{1})\times_{\tilde{Y}}\ldots\times_{\tilde{Y}}\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{A}_{n})=\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{A}_{1}\otimes_{\tilde{A}}\ldots\otimes_{\tilde{A}}\tilde{A}_{n}).

As
\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{A})\cap Y
is quasi-affine we find an open
\operatorname{Spec}(A)\subseteq\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{A})\cap Y
which contains all the
x_{i}
.
Then all maps
\operatorname{Spec}(A_{i})\to Y
factor through
\operatorname{Spec}(A)
hence

\mathcal{P}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\ldots\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\mathcal{P}_{n}=\mathcal{P}_{1}\times_{(\operatorname{Spec}(A),\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{A}))}\ldots\times_{(\operatorname{Spec}(A),\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{A}))}\mathcal{P}_{n}.

Therefore we are reduced to the case
\mathcal{Y}=(\operatorname{Spec}(A),\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{A}))
, and now the general case follows from the case where
n=2
.

Thus it suffices to consider the following situation. Let
\mathfrak{p}
and
\mathfrak{q}
be prime ideals of
A
.
Let
\mathcal{P}=(\operatorname{Spec}(B),\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{B}))
and
\mathcal{Q}=(\operatorname{Spec}(C),\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{C}))
be
\mathfrak{p}
-local and
\mathfrak{q}
-local objects, respectively,
with
\tilde{B}=B\times_{k_{B}}V_{B}
and
\tilde{C}=C\times_{k_{C}}V_{C}
as in Definition
6.1
.
Denote by
\mathfrak{m}_{B}\subset B
and
\mathfrak{m}_{C}\subset C
the maximal ideals.
By
[
HS20
, Theorem 6.3 and Theorem 6.4]
,
B\otimes_{A}C
is a product of henselian local
A
-algebras and the following holds:
let
D
be a factor of
B\otimes_{A}C
and denote by
\mathfrak{m}
its maximal ideal and
L=D/\mathfrak{m}
its residue field.

(1)

If the maps
B\to D
and
C\to D
are not local, then
L
is separably closed;

(2)

if
\varphi:B\to D
is local, then
the residue field extension
k_{B}\to L
is a separable algebraic extension.

In case
(1)
the natural map
\tilde{B}\otimes_{\tilde{A}}\tilde{C}\to D
is surjective.
Indeed, we have
\mathfrak{m}_{B}\subset\tilde{B}
and
\mathfrak{m}_{C}\subset\tilde{C}

and as
B\to D
and
C\to D
are not local we have
\mathfrak{m}_{B}\cdot\mathfrak{m}_{C}\cdot D=D
.
Thus
D
is integral over
\tilde{B}\otimes_{\tilde{A}}\tilde{C}
and is strictly henselian by
[
Art71
, Th.3.4(ii)]
.
Hence
(D,D)
is an
\mathfrak{r}
-local object, for
\mathfrak{r}=\mathfrak{m}_{D}\cap A\subset\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q}
, where we consider the trivial valuation on
k_{D}
,
and
(\operatorname{Spec}(D),\operatorname{Spec}(D))
is a component of
\mathcal{P}\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\mathcal{Q}
.

We consider case
(2)
.
Denote by
\mathfrak{m}
its maximal ideal of
D
and by
L=D/\mathfrak{m}
its residue field.
Let
w
be the unique valuation on
L
that extends the valuation
v
on
k_{B}
.
Thus
(L,w)
is a henselian valuation field and its valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{w}
is equal
to the integral closure of
V_{B}
in
L
, see, e.g.,
[
Bou98
, VI, §8, Proposition 6]
.
As
(k_{B},v)
is tamely closed so is
(L,w)
.
Let
\tilde{D}
be the integral closure of
\tilde{B}\otimes_{\tilde{A}}\tilde{C}
in
D
,
and let
W
be the image of the map
\tilde{D}\to D\to L
, so that we have the following commutative diagram

(6.3.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L.}

We claim

(6.3.2)

\mathfrak{m}\subset\tilde{D}.

Assuming (
6.3.2
) we directly get
\tilde{D}=D\times_{L}W
.
Moreover
W
contains
\mathcal{O}_{w}
. Indeed,
if
a\in L
is integral over
V_{B}
and
\tilde{a}\in D
is a lift of
a
,
then we find a monic polynomial
f\in\tilde{B}[X]
with
f(\tilde{a})\in\mathfrak{m}
which by the claim
is integral over
\tilde{B}\otimes_{\tilde{A}}\tilde{C}
, hence so is
\tilde{a}
, hence
a\in W
.
Thus
W
is a henselian valuation ring by
[
HS20
, Lemma 11.4]

and its valuation
w^{\prime}
is a generization of
w
, therefore
(L,w^{\prime})
is tamely closed.
Hence
(\operatorname{Spec}D,\operatorname{Spec}\tilde{D})
is a
x_{1}
-local object over
\mathcal{Y}
.
It remains to prove the claim (
6.3.2
).

Let
m\in\mathfrak{m}
. As
\varphi:B\to D
is integral, we find
a monic polynomial
f(X)=X^{n}+a_{1}X^{n-1}+\ldots a_{n}
in
B[X]
such that
f^{\varphi}(m)=0
,
where
f^{\varphi}=X^{n}+\varphi(a_{1})X^{n-1}+\ldots\varphi(a_{n})\in D[X]
.
Denote by
\bar{f}\in k_{B}[X]
the reduction of
f
modulo the maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{B}
.
As
0=f^{\varphi}(m)\equiv\varphi(a_{n})
mod
\mathfrak{m}
,
we have
a_{n}\in\varphi^{-1}(\mathfrak{m})=\mathfrak{m}_{B}
. Thus

\bar{f}=X^{e}\cdot\bar{g},

for some
e\geq 1
and
g\in k_{B}[X]
monic with
g(0)\neq 0
.
As
B
is henselian, there exist monic polynomials
h
,
g\in B[X]
with

f=hg,\quad h\equiv X^{e}\text{ mod }\mathfrak{m}_{B},\quad g\equiv\bar{g}\text{ mod }\mathfrak{m}_{B}.

It follows that the constant term of
g^{\varphi}
is a unit in
D
and hence so is
g^{\varphi}(m)
.
Thus
h^{\varphi}(m)=0
in
D
. As
h\in X^{e}+\mathfrak{m}_{B}[X]\subset\tilde{B}[X]
we find that
m
is integral over
\tilde{B}
,
hence
m\in\tilde{D}
. This yields claim (
6.3.2
) and completes the proof
of the lemma.
∎

The following theorem is analogous to
[
Art71
, Theorem 4.1]
while its proof is closer to the arguments given in the proof of

[
HS20
, Proposition 7.14 and Theorem 7.16]
.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
\mathcal{Y}=(Y,\tilde{Y})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
such that
\tilde{Y}
satisfies the property that every finite set of points is contained in an affine open.
Let
\mathcal{U}=(U,\tilde{U})\xrightarrow{(\varphi,\tilde{\varphi})}\mathcal{Y}
be a tame covering.
Then, for a tame covering
\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{U}^{\times_{\mathcal{Y}}n}
, there is a tame covering
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{U}
such that

{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}^{\times_{\mathcal{Y}}n}\to\mathcal{U}^{\times_{\mathcal{Y}}n}
factors through
\mathcal{V}
.

Proof.

Since
\tilde{U}
is quasi-compact and the open immersion
U\hookrightarrow\tilde{U}
is quasi-compact we find finitely many affine pairs

\mathcal{U}_{j}=(U_{j},\tilde{U}_{j})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{{\rm affine},t}
such that
U=\cup_{j}U_{j}
and
\tilde{U}=\cup_{j}\tilde{U}_{j}
are open coverings.
Thus the disjoint union
\sqcup_{j}\mathcal{U}_{j}
is an affine pair which tamely covers
\mathcal{U}
.
By Lemma
4.4
applied to this disjoint union,
we find a morphism
\mathcal{W}=(W,\tilde{W})\to\mathcal{U}
in
\widetilde{(X,\tilde{X})_{t}}
, which is a limit of tame coverings
\mathcal{W}_{i}=(W_{i},\tilde{W}_{i})\to\mathcal{U}
in
(X,\tilde{X})_{{\rm affine},t}
with
\mathcal{W}_{i}\in(X,\tilde{X})_{{\rm int},t}
,
such that
\mathcal{W}
is tamely acyclic. Note that every connected component
\mathcal{P}
of
\mathcal{W}
is an
x
-local object over
\mathcal{Y}
,
for some points
x\in Y
. Indeed, by Proposition
5.2
,
\mathcal{P}
satisfies the conditions
(2)
and
(3)
of Definition
6.1
, and
it suffices to check that
\mathcal{P}
satisfies
(1)
.
Denote by
x
the image in
Y
of the closed point of
P=\operatorname{Spec}S
.
Then we get a natural local morphism
\mathcal{O}_{Y,x}^{h}\to S
. It is ind-étale as
S
is a component of

\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{i}\mathcal{O}(W_{i})
and
W_{i}\to U
and
U\to Y
are étale.

Thus,
\mathcal{W}^{n}
(product over
\mathcal{Y}
) is a disjoint union of

\mathcal{P}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{Y}}...\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\mathcal{P}_{n}
, where
\mathcal{P}_{j}
are

x_{j}
-local objects for some
x_{j}\in Y
. Hence, by Lemma
6.3
and
Proposition
5.2
,
\mathcal{W}^{n}
is tamely acyclic.
Thus, for any tame covering
\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{U}^{n}
the map
\mathcal{W}^{n}\to\mathcal{U}^{n}
factors via
\mathcal{V}
.
Noting
\mathcal{W}_{i}^{n}\in(X,\tilde{X})_{{\rm int},t}
, Lemma
1.3
and Remark
1.4
imply that
there is
i_{0}
and a map
\mathcal{W}_{i_{0}}^{n}\to\mathcal{V}
in
(X,\tilde{X})_{{\rm affine},t}
that factors
\mathcal{W}_{i_{0}}^{n}\to\mathcal{U}^{n}
, hence by choosing

\mathcal{U}^{\prime}=\mathcal{W}_{i_{0}}
we conclude the proof.
∎

We are now ready to prove the comparison with Čech cohomology, analogously to
[
HS20
, Theorem 7.16]
and
[
Art71
, Corollary 4.2]
. Let us fix the notations: for
\mathcal{Y}\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
, we consider the Čech complex

\check{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{Y}_{t},-)\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}((X,\tilde{X})_{t},\mathbf{Ab})\to{\operatorname{\mathbf{Cpx}}}(\mathbf{Ab})\quad F\mapsto\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{Y}}F(\mathcal{U}^{\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\bullet}),

where the colimit runs over all the tame coverings
\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{Y}
. This is an exact functor, and its cohomology is the Čech cohomology
\check{H}^{q}(\mathcal{Y}_{t},-)

Theorem 6.5
.

Let
F
and
G
be presheaves of abelian groups on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
such that
a_{t}F\cong a_{t}G
, and let
\mathcal{Y}=(Y,\tilde{Y})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
such that
\tilde{Y}
satisfies the property that every finite set of points is contained in an affine open. Then there is an isomorphism of complexes

\check{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{Y}_{t},F)\cong\check{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{Y}_{t},G)

In particular, for
F
a sheaf of abelian groups on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
and
\mathcal{Y}
as above, the map comparing
tame Čech cohomology with the cohomology of the tame site

\check{H}^{q}(\mathcal{Y}_{t},F)\to H^{q}_{t}(\mathcal{Y},F)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Let
\iota\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X,\tilde{X})_{t},\mathbf{Ab})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}((X,\tilde{X})_{t},\mathbf{Ab})
be the inclusion. It is enough to show that the map
\check{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{Y}_{t},F)\to\check{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{Y}_{t},\iota a_{t}F)
is an isomorphism. Let
K
and
K^{\prime}
be the kernel and cokernel respectively of
F\to\iota a_{t}F
: we have exact sequences of presheaves

0\to K\to F\to F/K\to 0\quad 0\to F/K\to\iota a_{t}F\to K^{\prime}\to 0.

Thus, it is enough to show that if
a_{t}F=0
, then
\check{C}^{q}_{t}(\mathcal{Y},F)=0
for all
q
. Let
\alpha\in F(\mathcal{U}^{\times_{\mathcal{Y}}q})
for a tame covering
\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{Y}
. Then there is a tame covering
\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{U}^{\times_{\mathcal{Y}}q}
such that
\alpha\mapsto 0
in
F(\mathcal{V})
.
By Lemma
6.4
, there is a tame covering

\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{U}
such that
{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}^{\times_{\mathcal{Y}}q}\to\mathcal{U}^{\times_{\mathcal{Y}}q}
factors through
\mathcal{V}
.
Hence,
\alpha\mapsto 0
in

F({\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}^{\times_{\mathcal{Y}}q})
, which implies that it is zero in
\check{C}^{q}(\mathcal{Y},F)
.
The second part follows from the first: indeed let
\mathcal{H}^{q}(F)
be the presheaf
\mathcal{U}\to H^{q}(\mathcal{U}_{t},F)
on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
: since
a_{t}\mathcal{H}^{q}(F)=0
for
q>0
, by the previous part we have that
\check{H}^{p}(\mathcal{Y}_{t},\mathcal{H}^{q}(F))=0
for
q>0
, so the Čech-to-cohomology spectral sequence

E_{2}^{p,q}=\check{H}^{p}(\mathcal{Y}_{t},\mathcal{H}^{q}(F))\Rightarrow H^{p+q}(\mathcal{Y}_{t},F)

degenerates.

∎

7.
Computation of tame cohomology

We fix a quasi-compact open immersion
X\to\tilde{X}
of qcqs schemes.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
p
be a prime and let
F
be a sheaf of
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
-modules on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
. Let
(U,\tilde{U})\in\widetilde{(X,\tilde{X})}_{\tau}
connected and v-étale acyclic.
By Proposition
5.2
,
\mathcal{U}=(U,\tilde{U})=(\operatorname{Spec}A,\operatorname{Spec}\tilde{A})
where
A
is a henselian local ring with residue field
K
and
\tilde{A}=A\times_{K}\mathcal{O}_{v}
for a strictly henselian valuation
v
.
Write
\mathcal{U}
as the limit of a cofiltered system

\{\mathcal{U}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
in
(X,\tilde{X})_{\tau}
with

\mathcal{U}_{\lambda}=(\operatorname{Spec}A_{\lambda},\operatorname{Spec}\tilde{A}_{\lambda})
.
If the residue characteristic of
\mathcal{O}_{v}
is
p
, then

\varinjlim_{\lambda\in\Lambda}H^{i}((\mathcal{U}_{\lambda})_{t},F)=0,\quad\text{for }i\geq 1.

Proof.

By Theorem
6.5
, we have that

\varinjlim_{\lambda\in\Lambda}H^{i}((\mathcal{U}_{\lambda})_{t},F)=\varinjlim_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\varinjlim_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}\to\mathcal{U}_{\lambda}}\pi_{-i}F(\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}^{\times_{\mathcal{U}_{\lambda}}\bullet}),

where the colimit is indexed over tame covers of
\mathcal{U}_{\lambda}
.
By Lemma
5.4
, this is equal to

(7.1.1)

\varinjlim_{\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{U}}\pi_{-i}F(\mathcal{V}^{\times_{\mathcal{U}}\bullet}),

where the colimit is indexed over tame covers of
\mathcal{U}
in the sense of Definition
2.2

and
F
is left Kan extended to
\widetilde{(X,\tilde{X})}_{\tau}
.
By Remark
5.8
, we can further suppose that
\mathcal{V}
are of the form
(V,\tilde{V})=(\operatorname{Spec}(B),\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{B}))
with
A\to B
is a finite étale map of henselian local rings
associated to the residue field extension
L/K
which is Galois
and tame with respect to
v
and
\tilde{B}=B\times_{L}\mathcal{O}_{w}
with
w
the valuation on
L
extending
v
. Set

B_{n}:=B^{\otimes_{A}n},\quad V_{n}:=\operatorname{Spec}B_{n}\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{B}_{n}:=\tilde{B}^{\otimes_{\tilde{A}}n},\quad\tilde{V}_{n}:=\operatorname{Spec}\tilde{B}_{n}.

Let
B_{n}^{int}
be the integral closure of
\tilde{B}_{n}
in
B_{n}
and set
\tilde{V}_{n}^{int}=\operatorname{Spec}\tilde{B}_{n}^{int}
.
As
(V_{n},\tilde{V}_{n}^{int})\to(V_{n},\tilde{V}_{n})
is a quasi-modification, see Remark
4.2
,
the desired vanishing holds once we know that the following complex is exact

(7.1.2)

0\to F(U,\tilde{U})\to F(V,\tilde{V}^{\rm int})\to F(V_{2},\tilde{V}^{\rm int}_{2})\to F(V_{3},\tilde{V}_{3}^{\rm int})\to\cdots.

By
[
Bou98
, VI, §8, No. 6, Proposition 6]
the ring
\mathcal{O}_{w}
is also the integral closure of
\mathcal{O}_{v}
in
L
.
Hence the Galois group
{\rm Gal}(L/K)
is equal to the decomposition group
{\rm Aut}(\mathcal{O}_{w}/\mathcal{O}_{v})
.
Moreover, as the category of finite separable field extensions of
K
is equivalent to the category of finite local étale
A
-algebras,
we can identify the
A
-algebra automorphisms of
B
with
{\rm Gal}(L/K)
.
Hence
G:={\rm Gal}(L/K)={\rm Aut}(B/A)={\rm Aut}(\tilde{B}/\tilde{A})
.
As in
[
Mil80
, Example 2.6]
the isomorphism
B_{2}\to\prod_{\sigma\in G}B
,
b_{0}\otimes b_{1}\mapsto(\sigma(b_{0})b_{1})_{\sigma}

and induction give the isomorphism for
n\geq 2

\varphi_{n}:B_{n}\to\prod_{(\sigma_{0},\ldots,\sigma_{n-2})\in G^{n-1}}B

with

\varphi_{n}(b_{0}\otimes\ldots\otimes b_{n-1})_{(\sigma_{0},\ldots,\sigma_{n-2})}=(\sigma_{n-2}\cdots\sigma_{0})(b_{0})\cdot(\sigma_{n-2}\cdots\sigma_{1})(b_{1})\cdots\sigma_{n-2}(b_{n-2})\cdot b_{n-1}.

As
\tilde{B}
is integral over
\tilde{A}
, so is
\tilde{B}_{n}
, hence
\tilde{B}_{n}^{\rm int}
is the integral closure of
\tilde{A}
in
B_{n}

and thus
\varphi_{n}
restricts to an isomorphism

\tilde{B}_{n}^{\rm int}\to\prod_{(\sigma_{0},\ldots,\sigma_{n-2})}\tilde{B}.

We thus find isomorphisms

(V_{n},\tilde{V}_{n}^{\rm int})\cong(V,\tilde{V})\times G^{n-1}

as in
[
Mil80
, III, Example 2.6]
and can therefore identify the cohomology of (
7.1.2
)
with Galois cohomology

\pi_{-i}F(\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}^{\times_{\mathcal{U}_{\lambda}}\bullet})=H^{i}(G,F(V,\tilde{V})).

This vanishes as
F(V,\tilde{V})
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
-module and the order of
G
is invertible in
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
by tameness.
∎

A version of the following theorem for discretely ringed adic spaces with
p
-torsion coefficients
holds by
[
Hüb21
, Proposition 8.5]
and
[
Hübb
, Corollary 5.7]
.
The method of proof presented below differs from the one in
loc. cit.

Theorem 7.2
.

Let
X\hookrightarrow\tilde{X}
be a quasi-compact open immersion of qcqs schemes.
For
\mathcal{V}:=(V,\tilde{V})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
, let
j^{\mathcal{V}}:(V,\tilde{V})_{t}\to\tilde{V}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
be the morphisms of sites induced by the functor
\tilde{V}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}\to(V,\tilde{V})_{t}
sending
(\tilde{W}\to\tilde{V})
to
(V\times_{\tilde{V}}\tilde{W},\tilde{W})
.
Let
F
be a sheaf of abelian groups on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
such that the following condition is satisfied:

(p)

for every
(U,\tilde{U})\to(X,\tilde{X})
in
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
, the stalk of the Zariski sheaf on
\tilde{U}
given by
W\mapsto F(W\cap U,W)

at any point
x\in\tilde{U}
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p_{x})}
-module, where

p_{x}
is the exponential characteristic of
\kappa(x)
.

Then, for
\mathcal{U}=(U,\tilde{U})
in
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
, we have canonical isomorphisms

H^{i}(\mathcal{U}_{t},F)\cong H^{i}(\mathcal{U}_{{\operatorname{v\acute{e}t}}},\nu_{*}F)\cong\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{U}}H^{i}(\tilde{V}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}},j^{\mathcal{W}}_{*}F)\cong\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{U}}H^{i}(\tilde{V}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}},j^{\mathcal{V}}_{*}F),\quad i\geq 0,

where the first colimit runs along all quasi-modifications
\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{U}
with
\mathcal{V}
integral, and the second colimit runs along all admissible blow-ups
\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{U}
.

Proof.

Note that
j^{\mathcal{V}}
is the composition of the morphism of sites

\nu:(V,\tilde{V})_{t}\to(V,\tilde{V})_{{\operatorname{v\acute{e}t}}}

corresponding to the inclusion functor and the morphism of sites

\lambda^{\mathcal{V}}\colon(V,\tilde{V})_{{\operatorname{v\acute{e}t}}}\to\tilde{V}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}

defined by the functor

\tilde{V}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}\to(V,\tilde{V})_{{\operatorname{v\acute{e}t}}}:\tilde{W}/\tilde{V}\mapsto(\tilde{W}\times_{\tilde{V}}V,\tilde{W})
.
By Lemma
3.5
, we have

H^{i}(\mathcal{U}_{t},F)=H^{i}(\mathcal{U}_{{\operatorname{v\acute{e}t}}},R\nu_{*}F)=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{U}}H^{i}(\tilde{V}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}},\lambda^{\mathcal{V}}_{*}R\nu_{*}F)=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{U}}H^{i}(\tilde{V}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}},\lambda^{\mathcal{W}}_{*}R\nu_{*}F).

Hence, it suffices to show
R^{i}\nu_{*}F=0
for
i\geq 1
.
By Remark
5.6
,
this follows from Proposition
7.1

and the fact that the assumptions of
loc. cit.
are satisfied by condition
(p)
. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 7.3
.

Note that the condition
(p)
of Theorem
7.2
is satisfied
if
F=\Omega^{q,t}
, see
8.4
(4)
,
or
F=\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q,t}
(see Definition
9.5
below). Also it holds if
\tilde{X}
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
-scheme
and
F
is any tame sheaf of
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
-modules.

8.
Construction of tame sheaves

Let
X\hookrightarrow\tilde{X}
be a quasi-compact open immersion of qcqs schemes.
We give a method to extend étale sheaves defined over
X_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
to sheaves on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
.

8.1
.

For
\mathcal{X}=(X,\tilde{X})
, we let
\mathrm{Val}^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathcal{X}}
be the category whose objects are triples
(L,w,\varepsilon)
,
where
L
is a finite separable field extension of a residue field
k(x)
of a point of
X
,

w
is a valuation on
L
, and

\varepsilon\colon\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{w})\to\tilde{X}
is a morphism, which restricts to the map

\operatorname{Spec}L\to\operatorname{Spec}k(x)\to X
,
and morphisms
(L,w,\varepsilon)\to(L^{\prime},w^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime})
are given by valued extensions
(the compatibility with
\varepsilon
is automatic).
Note that for any
(x,v,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Spa}(X,\tilde{X})
and any finite separable extension of valuation fields

(L,w)/(k(x),v)
uniquely determines an element
(L,w,\varepsilon^{\prime})\in\mathrm{Val}^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathcal{X}}
with
\varepsilon^{\prime}
equal to the composition

\varepsilon^{\prime}:\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{w}\to\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{v}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}\tilde{X}
.

For
(L,w,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Val}^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathcal{X}}
we set

(8.1.1)

\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,L}=\varinjlim_{\operatorname{Spec}L\to U\to X}\mathcal{O}(U)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{O}^{h}_{\tilde{X},L,w}=\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,L}\times_{L}\mathcal{O}_{w},

where the direct limit is over all étale maps
U\to X
which factor
\operatorname{Spec}L\to X
.
Note that
\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,L}
is the unique henselian local ring with residue field
L
which is finite étale over
\mathcal{O}_{X,x}^{h}
,
corresponding to the field extension
L/k(x)
. In particular the association

(L,w,\varepsilon)\mapsto\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,L}
defines a functor from
\mathrm{Val}^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathcal{X}}
to the category of henselian local rings which are
ind-étale over
X
.

Let
F
be a sheaf on
X_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
. We write
F(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,L}):=\varinjlim_{\operatorname{Spec}L\to U\to X}F(U)
.
Let

\beta=\{F_{w}\subset F(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,L})\}_{(L,w,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Val}^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathcal{X}}},

be a collection of subsets satisfying the following condition

(
\beta
1)

for any
(L,w,\varepsilon)\to(L_{1},w_{1},\varepsilon_{1})
in
\mathrm{Val}^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathcal{X}}
,
the pullback map
F(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,L})\to F(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,L_{1}})

restricts to
F_{w}\to F_{w_{1}}
.

For
(U,\tilde{U})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
we define

F_{\beta}(U,\tilde{U}):=\left\{a\in F(U)\,\middle|\,\begin{minipage}[199.16928pt]for all $(x,v,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Spa}(U,\tilde{U})$
there exists a finite tame extension $(L,w)/(k(x),v)$, such that $a_{L}\in F_{w}$\end{minipage}\right\},

where
a_{L}
denotes the pullback of
a\in F(U)
along
\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,L}\to U
.

Note that by Lemma
2.1
and
(

β

1)
it suffices to consider in the definition of
F_{\beta}(U,\tilde{U})

only the finite tame
Galois
extensions
(L,w)/(k(x),v)
.

Proposition 8.2
.

Assumptions as in
8.1
above.
The assignment
(U,\tilde{U})\mapsto F_{\beta}(U,\tilde{U})
defines a sheaf on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
.

Proof.

We start by showing that
F_{\beta}
is a presheaf.
Let
(u,\tilde{u}):(U^{\prime},\tilde{U}^{\prime})\to(U,\tilde{U})
be a morphism in
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
and take
a\in F_{\beta}(U,\tilde{U})
.
Let
(y,v,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Spa}(U^{\prime},\tilde{U}^{\prime})
. Set
x:=u(y)\in U
and denote by
v_{x}
the restriction of
v
to
k(x)
.
Note that
\mathcal{O}_{v_{x}}=\mathcal{O}_{v}\cap k(x)
, the intersection taken inside
k(y)
.
Hence
\varepsilon:\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{v}\to\tilde{U}^{\prime}\to\tilde{U}
factors uniquely via a map
\varepsilon_{x}:\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{v_{x}}\to\tilde{U}
.
We obtain a point
(x,v_{x},\varepsilon_{x})\in\mathrm{Spa}(U,\tilde{U})
.
By definition there exists a finite tame extension

(L,w)/(k(x),v_{x})
such that
a_{L}\in F_{w}
.
Denote by
L_{1}
the composition field of
L
and
k(y)
in a separable closure of
k(x)
and choose a valuation
v_{1}

on
L_{1}
extending
v
. Then the extension
(L_{1},v_{1})/(k(y),v)
is tame,
by Lemma
2.1
(2)
.
Now
u^{*}(a)_{L_{1}}
, the image of
u^{*}(a)
under
F(U^{\prime})\to F(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{U^{\prime},L_{1}})
,
is equal to the image of the pullback of
a_{L}
under
F(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{U,L})\to F(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{U^{\prime},L_{1}})
.
As
a_{L}\in F_{w}
we find
u^{*}(a)_{L_{1}}\in F_{w_{1}}
by
(

β

1)
in
8.1
.
This shows
u^{*}(a)\in F_{\beta}(U^{\prime},\tilde{U}^{\prime})
. Hence
F_{\beta}
is a presheaf.

As
F
is an étale sheaf on
X
,
F_{\beta}
will be a sheaf on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
if we show the following:
Let
\{(U_{i},\tilde{U}_{i})\to(U,\tilde{U})\}_{i\in I}
be a tame covering in
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
and let
a\in F(U)
,
then

(8.2.1)

a_{|U_{i}}\in F_{\beta}(U_{i},\tilde{U}_{i}),\quad\text{for all }i\,\Longrightarrow\,a\in F_{\beta}(U,\tilde{U}).

Let
(x,v,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Spa}(U,\tilde{U})
. By definition of tame covering we find an index
i
and a point

(y,w,\varepsilon^{\prime})\in\mathrm{Spa}(U_{i},\tilde{U}_{i})
over
(x,v,\varepsilon)
such that
(k(y),w)/(k(x),v)
is a finite tame extension.
As
a_{|U_{i}}\in F_{\beta}(U_{i},\tilde{U}_{i})
we find a finite tame extension
(L,w_{1})/(k(y),w)
such that

(a_{|U_{i}})_{L}\in F_{w_{1}}
.
Hence, we get (
8.2.1
).
∎

Remark 8.3
.

By definition, for all
F
,
\beta
as in
8.1
above we have a pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\beta}(U,\tilde{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}F_{w}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}F(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{U,L})}

where:

•

the product ranges over all elements
(x,v,\varepsilon)
of
\mathrm{Spa}(U,\tilde{U})

•

the colimit ranges over all
(k(x),v)\subseteq(L,w)
finite tame.

This implies that if
\varphi\colon F\to G
is a map of sheaves on
X_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}
and
F
and
G
are equipped with
\beta
-families
\beta_{F}:=\{F_{w}\}
and
\beta_{G}:=\{G_{w}\}
such that for every
(U,\tilde{U})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
and every
(x,v,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Spa}(U,\tilde{U})
there is a cofinal system of tame extensions
S_{(x,v,\varepsilon)}:=\{(k(x),v)\subseteq(L,w)\}
such that the map
\varphi:F(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,L})\to G(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,L})
restricts to a map

\varphi_{w}\colon F_{w}\to G_{w}
for all
(L,w)\in S_{x,v,\varepsilon}
, then
\varphi
induces a map
F_{\beta_{F}}\to G_{\beta_{G}}

of sheaves on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
, denoted by
\varphi
as well.

Example 8.4
.

In the following
(L,w,\varepsilon)
is always in
\mathrm{Val}^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathcal{X}}
.
We set
A_{L}=\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,L}
and
A_{L,w}=\mathcal{O}^{h}_{\tilde{X},L,w}
, see (
8.1.1
) for notation

(1)

Let
F
be a sheaf on
X_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
. Set

F_{w,0}=F(A_{L})
, if
(L,w,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Val}^{\mathrm{s}}_{(X,X)}
, and
F_{w,0}=0
, else.
Set
F_{w,{\rm triv}}=F(A_{L})
, for all
(L,w,\varepsilon)
. Then
\beta_{0}=\{F_{w,0}\}_{(L,w,\varepsilon)}

and
\beta_{\rm triv}=\{F_{w,{\rm triv}}\}
are families as in
8.1
and we have

\imath_{*}F_{\beta_{0}}=j_{!}F\quad\text{and}\quad\imath_{*}F_{\beta_{\rm triv}}=j_{*}F,

where
j:X\to\tilde{X}
is the open immersion and
\imath:(X,\tilde{X})_{t}\to\tilde{X}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
is the morphism of sites induced by the functor

\tilde{U}\mapsto(\tilde{U}\times_{\tilde{X}}X,\tilde{U})
.

(2)

Let
F
be as above and assume there is a presheaf
\widetilde{F}
on
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}_{\tilde{X}}

extending
F
. Then we can define
\beta_{\widetilde{F}}=\{\widetilde{F}(A_{L,w})\subset F(A_{L})\}_{(L,w,\varepsilon)}
.
We get a sheaf
F_{\beta_{\widetilde{F}}}
on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
. Notice that the resulting sheaf
\iota_{*}F_{\beta_{\widetilde{F}}}

may be different from
\widetilde{F}_{|\tilde{X}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}
, e.g.,
for
\widetilde{F}=\mathcal{O}
, the structure sheaf on
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}_{\tilde{X}}
,
the sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{|\tilde{X}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}
is different from
\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\beta_{\widetilde{F}}}=\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}^{t}
, where
\mathcal{O}^{t}
is defined in
(4)
, see Lemma
8.5
below.

(3)

Given a collection
\beta
as in (
8.1
) on a sheaf
F
, we can
define a new family

\beta^{div}=\{F^{div}_{w}\subset F(A_{L})\}_{(L,w,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Val}^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathcal{X}}}

by setting

F^{div}_{w}:=\begin{cases}F_{w}&\begin{minipage}[172.5pt]if $\operatorname{Spec}L$ maps to a generic point of $X$ \\
and $w$ is a discrete valuation,
\end{minipage}\\
F(A_{L})&\text{else}.\end{cases}

If
\beta
satisfies
(

β

1)
, then so does
\beta^{div}
. Here note that if
(L,w,\varepsilon)\to(L^{\prime},w^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime})
is a morphism in

\mathrm{Val}^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathcal{X}}
and
w
is discrete, then
w^{\prime}
is discrete as well. We denote by
F^{div}_{\beta}:=F_{\beta^{div}}

the corresponding tame sheaf. For sections in
F^{div}_{\beta}(U,\tilde{U})
we only put conditions induced by
\beta

along tame extensions of discrete valuations on the generic points of
U
with center in
\tilde{U}
, hence

F_{\beta}\subset F^{div}_{\beta}
.

(4)

Let
F=\Omega^{q}
be the étale sheaf of
q
th absolute differential forms on
X_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
.
Denote by
\Omega^{*}_{A_{L,w}}(\log)
the graded
\Omega^{*}_{A_{L,w}}
-subalgebra of

\Omega^{*}_{A_{L}}
generated by
{\operatorname{dlog}}(A_{L}^{\times})
.
Note
\Omega^{q}_{A_{L,w}}\subset\Omega^{q}_{A_{L,w}}(\log)
.
Set

\beta_{\log}:=\{\Omega^{q}_{A_{L,w}}(\log)\subset\Omega^{q}_{A_{L}}\}_{(L,w,\varepsilon)}.

Then
\beta_{\log}
satisfies
(

β

1)
in
8.1
and we get a sheaf

(8.4.1)

\Omega^{q,t}:=\Omega^{q}_{\beta_{\log}}\quad\text{on }(X,\tilde{X})_{t}.

Note for
q=0
, we write
\mathcal{O}^{t}=\Omega^{0,t}
. This is a special case of
(2)
,
where we take
\widetilde{F}
to be the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}
on
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}_{\tilde{X}}
.
Note also that the differential of the de Rham complex induces a well-defined differential

d:\Omega^{q,t}\to\Omega^{q+1,t}
giving rise to a complex of
sheaves
\Omega^{\bullet,t}
on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
. Using
(3)
above we get an inlcusion of sheaves on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}

\Omega^{q,t}\subset\Omega^{q,div}:=(\Omega^{q}_{\beta_{\log}})^{div}.

Furthermore, given a morphism
\tilde{X}\to S
we can similarly define the complex

\Omega^{\bullet,t}_{/S}=\Omega^{\bullet}_{/S,\beta_{\log/S}}
, where

\beta_{\log/S}=\{\Omega^{q}_{A_{L,w}/S}(\log)\subset\Omega^{q}_{A_{L}/S}\}_{(L,w,\varepsilon)}
.

(5)

Let
W_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}
denote the
p
-typical de Rham–Witt complex,
it is defined as an étale sheaf on all schemes, by
[
Hes15
]
.
Denote by
W_{n}\Omega^{*}_{A_{L,w}}(\log)
the graded
W_{n}\Omega^{*}_{A_{L,w}}
-subalgebra of
W_{n}\Omega^{*}_{A_{L}}
generated
by
{\operatorname{dlog}}[a]
,
a\in{A_{L}}^{\times}
, where
[-]:A_{L}\to W_{n}(A_{L})
denotes the multiplicative lift.
Note
W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{A_{L,w}}\subset W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{A_{L,w}}(\log)
.
Then
\beta_{\log,n}=\{W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{A_{L,w}}(\log)\subset W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{A_{L}}\}_{(L,w,\varepsilon)}

satisfies the assumption from
8.1
and we get sheaves

W_{n}\Omega^{q,t}:=W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\beta_{\log,n}}\subset W_{n}\Omega^{q,div}:=(W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\beta_{\log,n}})^{div}\quad\text{on }(X,\tilde{X})_{t}.

Note that the differential, the restriction map, as well as Frobenius and Verschiebung on the de Rham–Witt complex
induce well-defined maps on
W_{n}\Omega^{q,t}
and
W_{n}\Omega^{q,div}
. In particular
W_{\bullet}\Omega^{\bullet,t}

and
W_{n}\Omega^{\bullet,div}
are Witt complexes in the sense of
[
Hes15
, Definition 4]
.

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
\mathcal{O}^{t}=\Omega^{0,t}
and
\mathcal{O}^{div}=\Omega^{0,div}
in the notation
from
8.4
(4)
.
For all
(U,\tilde{U})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
we have

\mathcal{O}^{t}(U,\tilde{U})=\mathcal{O}(\tilde{U}^{\rm int}),

where
\tilde{U}^{\rm int}
denotes the integral closure of
\tilde{U}
in
U
.
Moreover, if
\tilde{U}
is a Nagata scheme and
U
is normal, then we have

\mathcal{O}^{t}(U,\tilde{U})=\mathcal{O}^{div}(U,\tilde{U}).

Proof.

By Lemma
2.7
we can assume
\tilde{U}=\tilde{U}^{\rm int}
.
We notice that both sides of the first equality in the statement
are contained in
\mathcal{O}(U)
and that this ”
\supset
” inclusion holds by definition of the left hand side.
As both sides are sheaves on
\tilde{U}
it suffices to check the other inclusion for
\tilde{U}
affine.
As an affine open cover
U=\cup_{i}U_{i}
induces an affine open cover

\tilde{U}=\cup_{i}\tilde{U}_{i}^{\rm int}
we can assume
(U,\tilde{U})=(\operatorname{Spec}A,\operatorname{Spec}\tilde{A})
with
\tilde{A}
integrally closed in
A
.
Let
x\in A\setminus\tilde{A}
.

Claim 8.6
.

There exist a prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}
in
A
and a valuation
v
on
K={\operatorname{Frac}}(A/\mathfrak{p})
such that

\tilde{A}\to A\to K
factors through
\mathcal{O}_{v}
and
v(x)<0
.

Admitting the claim, we also have
w(x)<0
for all (tame) extensions
(L,w)/(K,v)
of valuation fields and
x
cannot lie in
\mathcal{O}^{t}(U,\tilde{U})
, which completes the proof of the lemma.

We prove the claim. Denote by
C=\tilde{A}[1/x]
the subring of
A_{x}
generated by the image of
\tilde{A}
and
1/x
.
As
x
is not integral over
\tilde{A}
it follows from
[
Bou98
, VI, §1, no. 2, Lemma 1]
,
that
A_{x}
is not the zero-ring and that there exists a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}\subset C
such that

1/x\in\mathfrak{m}
and
\mathfrak{m}\cap\tilde{A}
is a maximal ideal of
\tilde{A}
.
Consider the localization
C_{\mathfrak{m}}
of
C
. By the flatness of
C_{\mathfrak{m}}
over
C
,
the inclusion
C\hookrightarrow A_{x}
induces an injection of rings

C_{\mathfrak{m}}=C\otimes_{C}C_{\mathfrak{m}}\hookrightarrow A_{x}\otimes_{C}C_{\mathfrak{m}}
. As
C_{\mathfrak{m}}
is not the zero ring, the ring

A_{x}\otimes_{C}C_{\mathfrak{m}}
is not zero and hence has a prime ideal;
it corresponds to a prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}
of
A
, which does not contain
x
and has empty intersection with

C\setminus\mathfrak{m}
. Set
\mathfrak{p}_{0}:=C\cap\mathfrak{p}A_{x}
. By construction we have
\mathfrak{p}_{0}\subset\mathfrak{m}
, and in fact
this inclusion is strict as else we would have
1/x\in\mathfrak{p}A_{x}
since
1/x\in\mathfrak{m}
.
Thus,
C/\mathfrak{p}_{0}
is not a field.
Set
K_{0}:={\operatorname{Frac}}(C/\mathfrak{p}_{0})\subset K:={\operatorname{Frac}}(A_{x}/\mathfrak{p}A_{x})={\operatorname{Frac}}(A/\mathfrak{p})
.
Let
v_{0}
be a valuation on
K_{0}
such that
C/\mathfrak{p}_{0}\subset\mathcal{O}_{v_{0}}\subset K_{0}

and that
\mathfrak{m}_{v_{0}}\cap C/\mathfrak{p}_{0}

is the image of
\mathfrak{m}
in
C/\mathfrak{p}_{0}
.
Let
v
be a valuation on
K
extending
v_{0}
.
Thus we obtain a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C/\mathfrak{p}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{v_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{v}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{x}/\mathfrak{p}A_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K.}

Note
v(x)=v_{0}(x)<0
since
1/x\in\mathfrak{m}
so that its image in
C/\mathfrak{p}_{0}
is in
\mathfrak{m}_{v_{0}}
.
This completes the proof of the claim.

For the last statement, we observe that as
\tilde{U}
is Nagata and
U
is normal
the integral closure
\tilde{U}^{int}
is finite over
\tilde{U}
and hence is locally noetherian again. As
U
is normal so is
\tilde{U}^{int}
. We may therefore assume that
\tilde{U}^{int}
is noetherian, integral and normal.
Hence

\mathcal{O}^{t}(U,\tilde{U})=\mathcal{O}(\tilde{U}^{\mathrm{int}})=\bigcap_{x\in{\tilde{U}}^{int,(1)}}\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{U}^{int},x}=\mathcal{O}^{div}(U,\tilde{U}),

where
\tilde{U}^{int,(1)}
is the set of 1-codimensional points in
U^{int}
and
where the last equality follows from
\mathcal{O}^{div}(U,\tilde{U})=\mathcal{O}^{div}(U,\tilde{U}^{int})
and the definition of
\mathcal{O}^{div}
,
see
(3)
.
∎

Remark 8.7
.

Combining Lemma
8.5
and Theorem
7.2
, we conclude that for
S
a qcqs scheme
and
X
a normal separated
S
-scheme of finite type and
\tilde{X}
any
S
-compactification, the following isomorphism holds for all
i\geq 0
:

H^{i}((X,\tilde{X})_{t},\mathcal{O}^{t})\cong\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{\tilde{Y}}H^{i}(\tilde{Y}_{{\operatorname{Zar}}},\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{Y}}),

where the colimit is over all quasi-modifications
(X,\tilde{Y})\to(X,\tilde{X})
with
\tilde{Y}
normal. An analogous result for certain adic spaces was obtained in

[
Hüb21
, Corollary 13.6]
.

9.
Tame big de Rham–Witt complex

In this section we define the tame big de Rham–Witt complex for any quasi-compact open immersion
X\subset\tilde{X}
of qcqs schemes,
see Definition
9.5
, generalizing the tame sheaves
\Omega^{\bullet,t}_{-/\mathbb{Z}}
and
W_{n}\Omega^{\bullet,t}

introduced in
8.4
. We compute the big de Rham–Witt complex for local rings in the tame site,
see Proposition
9.13
below. In the next section
we will use these results to analyse the tame cohomology of
the tame
p
-typical de Rham–Witt complex in positive characteristic.

9.1
.

We denote by
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{A}
the degree
q
-part of the big de Rham–Witt complex of a ring
A
at the truncation set
S
,
defined by Hesselholt in
[
Hes15
, Theorem B]
. If
X
is a scheme, then we denote by
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{X}

the étale sheaf on
(\rm Sch/X)_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
associated to the presheaf

(T\to X)\mapsto\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})}.

For
S
a finite truncation set, it follows from the fact that
\mathbb{W}_{S}(A)\to\mathbb{W}_{S}(B)
is étale if
A\to B
is
(see
[
vdK86
, Theorem 2.4]
or
[
Bor11
, Theorem B]
, cf. also
[
Hes15
, Theorem 1.25]
),
étale base change
[
Hes15
, Theorem C]
, and descent that

\Gamma(\operatorname{Spec}A,\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{X})=\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{A},\quad\text{for any }\operatorname{Spec}A\to X.

In particular
\mathbb{W}_{\{1\}}\Omega^{q}_{X}
is the usual sheaf of absolute Kähler differentials of degree
q
.
Note however, that
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{*}_{A}
is not a complex in general as
dd(a)={\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]da
,
for
a\in\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{*}_{A}

and
{\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]
does not vanish in general.
If
X
is an
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-scheme, then

\mathbb{W}_{\{1,p,\ldots,p^{n-1}\}}\Omega^{q}_{X}=W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{X}

is the degree
q
and length
n
part of the
p
-typical de Rham–Witt complex of Bloch–Deligne–Illusie
[
Ill79
]
,
see
[
Hes15
, Remark 4.2(c)]
. Furthermore,
in this case the big de Rham–Witt complex at a general truncation set decomposes
as a product of
p
-typical ones, see
[
HM01
, Corollary 1.2.6]
.

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
A
be a ring and
I\subset A
an ideal.
Then the kernel of the natural surjection
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{*}_{A}\to\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{*}_{A/I}
is the graded ideal

J_{S}^{*}
which in degree
q
is given by

J_{S}^{q}:=\mathbb{W}_{S}(I)\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{A}+d(\mathbb{W}_{S}(I)\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}),

where
\mathbb{W}_{S}(I):=\operatorname{Ker}(\mathbb{W}_{S}(A)\to\mathbb{W}_{S}(A/I))
.

Proof.

This is a version of
[
GH06
, Lemma 2.4]
for the big de Rham–Witt complex.
Note that by the Leibniz rule
[
Hes15
, Definition 4.1 (i)]
we have

(9.2.1)

J_{S}^{q}=\mathbb{W}_{S}(I)\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{A}+d(\mathbb{W}_{S}(I))\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}

We use this presentation of
J^{q}_{S}
in the following.
We get a functor from truncation sets to anticommutative graded rings

S\mapsto\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{*}_{A}/J_{S}^{*}
and as in
loc. cit.
it suffices to show that the differential
d
, Frobenius
F_{n}
, and Verschiebung
V_{n}

on
\mathbb{W}_{-}\Omega^{*}_{A}
induce well-defined maps on
\mathbb{W}_{-}\Omega^{*}_{A}/J_{-}^{*}
. Indeed, in this case
the natural surjection of Witt complexes
\mathbb{W}_{-}\Omega^{*}_{A}\to\mathbb{W}_{-}\Omega^{*}_{A/I}
induces a surjection
of Witt-complexes
\mathbb{W}_{-}\Omega^{*}_{A}/J_{-}^{*}\to\mathbb{W}_{-}\Omega^{*}_{A/I}
and the universal property of the de Rham–Witt complex
yields the inverse map.

Every element in
J_{S}^{*}
can be written as a sum of elements

V_{n}([a])\cdot\beta\quad\text{and}\quad dV_{n}([a])\cdot\beta,

where
\beta\in\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{*}_{A}
,
a\in I
,
[a]\in\mathbb{W}_{S/n}(I)
denotes the multiplicative lift of
a
, and
V_{n}

is the
n
th Verschiebung. By
[
Hes15
, Definition 4.1 (i)]
we have

(9.2.2)

d(V_{n}([a])\beta)=dV_{n}([a])\beta+V_{n}([a])d\beta

and

d(dV_{n}([a])\beta)={\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]dV_{n}([a])\beta-dV_{n}([a])d\beta=-dV_{n}([a])({\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]\beta+d\beta).

Hence
d(J^{*}_{S})\subset J^{*+1}_{S}
. As
F_{m}:\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{*}_{A}\to\mathbb{W}_{S/m}\Omega^{*}_{A}
(
m\geq 1
) is a ring map
which restricts to a map
\mathbb{W}_{S}(I)\to\mathbb{W}_{S/m}(I)
the containment
F_{m}(J^{*}_{S})\subset J^{*}_{S/m}
follows from

(9.2.3)

\displaystyle F_{m}dV_{n}([a])

\displaystyle=idF_{m_{1}}V_{n_{1}}([a])+jF_{m_{1}}V_{n_{1}}d([a])+(c-1){\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]F_{m_{1}}V_{n_{1}}([a])

\displaystyle=idV_{n_{1}}([a^{m_{1}}])+jV_{n_{1}}([a]^{m_{1}-1}d[a])+(c-1)V_{n_{1}}([a]^{m_{1}}){\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]

\displaystyle=idV_{n_{1}}([a^{m_{1}}])+jV_{n_{1}}([a]^{m_{1}-1})dV_{n_{1}}([a])+(c-1-jn_{1}+j)V_{n_{1}}([a]^{m_{1}}){\operatorname{dlog}}[-1],

where
c={\rm gcd}(m,n)
,
m=cm_{1}
,
n=cn_{1}
, and
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfy
im+nj=c
.
Here the first equality is the third equation in
[
Hes15
, Lemma 4.3]
,
the second equality uses
V_{r}F_{s}=F_{s}V_{r}
, for
(r,s)=1
,
F_{s}([a])=[a]^{s}
, and
F_{s}(d[a])=[a]^{s-1}d[a]
,
and the third equality uses
F_{r}dV_{r}([a])=d[a]+(r-1){\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]\cdot[a]
,
F_{r}({\operatorname{dlog}}[-1])={\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]
,
and
V_{r}(F_{r}(x)y)=xV_{r}(y)
, see
[
Hes15
, Definition 4.1, Lemma 4.3]
.
(If
m_{1}=1
we can choose
i=1
and
j=0
and deduce from the second equality that
F_{m}dV_{n}([a])
lies in
J_{S/m}^{1}
.)
It remains to show
V_{m}(J^{*}_{S})\subset J^{*}_{T}
for any truncation set
T
with
T/m=S
.
From
(
9.2.2
)
and
V_{m}d=mdV_{m}
we see that it suffices to show

V_{m}(a\beta)\in J^{*}_{T}
, for any
a\in\mathbb{W}_{S}(I)
. As by construction of the big de Rham–Witt complex a form
\beta

is a sum of elements
b_{0}db_{1}\cdots db_{q}
with
b_{i}\in\mathbb{W}_{S}(A)
and since
ab_{0}\in\mathbb{W}_{S}(I)

it suffices to consider elements
\beta=db_{1}\cdots db_{q}
.
As
db_{i}=F_{m}dV_{m}(b_{i})-(m-1)b_{i}{\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]
and
{\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]\cdot{\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]=0

[
Hes15
, Lemma 4.3]
we find

\displaystyle adb_{1}\cdots db_{q}

\displaystyle=aF_{m}\left(dV_{m}(b_{1})\cdots dV_{m}(b_{q})\right)

\displaystyle-(m-1)\sum_{i=1}^{q}ab_{i}F_{m}\left(dV_{m}(b_{1})\cdots\underbrace{{\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]}_{i\text{th spot}}\cdots dV_{m}(b_{q})\right).

Thus
V_{m}(adb_{1}\cdots db_{q})\in J_{T}^{*}
follows from the formula
V_{m}(xF_{m}(y))=V_{m}(x)y
.
This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

In the following, the
truncation set
S
is always finite
.

9.3
.

Let
(A,\mathfrak{m}_{A})
be a local ring with residue field
K=A/\mathfrak{m}_{A}
. Let
v
be a valuation on
K

with valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{v}
and set
\tilde{A}=A\times_{K}\mathcal{O}_{v}
.
Note that
\mathfrak{m}_{A}
is a prime ideal of
\tilde{A}
.
In this situation, we define
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)

to be the degree
q
-part of the graded
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{*}_{\tilde{A}}
-subalgebra of
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{*}_{A}
generated
by forms
{\operatorname{dlog}}[a]
, with
a\in A^{\times}
, where
[a]\in\mathbb{W}_{S}(A)
denotes the multiplicative lift.

This extends the definition of
\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)
from
8.4
(4)
.
Note that
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{0}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)=\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{A})
.
Moreover,
d
,
F_{n}
, and
V_{n}
restrict to maps on
\mathbb{W}_{-}\Omega^{*}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)
.
Indeed for
F_{n}
this follows from the fact that
F_{n}
is a ring map and the formula
F_{n}{\operatorname{dlog}}[a]={\operatorname{dlog}}[a]
;
for
V_{n}
this follows from the latter formula and the
F_{n}
-linearity of
V_{n}
;
for
d
this follows from the Leibniz rule and the formula

(9.3.1)

d{\operatorname{dlog}}[a]=-{\operatorname{dlog}}[a]{\operatorname{dlog}}[a]+{\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]{\operatorname{dlog}}[a]=2{\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]{\operatorname{dlog}}[a]=0,

where the first equality uses Leibniz rule and
dd[a]={\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]d[a]
and the second
equality uses
d[a]d[a]=-{\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]F_{2}d[a]=-[a]{\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]d[a]
.

Lemma 9.4
.

Let
A,\mathfrak{m}_{A},K=A/\mathfrak{m}_{A},v,\tilde{A}
be as in
9.3
above. Let
\tilde{A}\to\tilde{B}
be an étale local homomorphism of local rings.
Then
\tilde{B}/\mathfrak{m}_{A}\tilde{B}
is a valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{w}
, which is unramified over
\mathcal{O}_{v}
and
\tilde{B}=B\times_{L}\mathcal{O}_{w}
,
where
B=\tilde{B}\otimes_{\tilde{A}}A
is a local ring with maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{A}B
and residue field
L={\operatorname{Frac}}(\mathcal{O}_{w})
. Moreover,

\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{B})
is a faithfully flat étale
\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{A})
-algebra and
multiplication induces a canonical isomorphism

(9.4.1)

\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{B})\otimes_{\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{A})}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{B}}(\log).

Proof.

By assumption
\mathcal{O}_{v}=\tilde{A}/\mathfrak{m}_{A}\to\tilde{B}/\mathfrak{m}_{A}\tilde{B}
is an étale local homomorphism and it follows from the arguments
in
[
GR03
, Remark 6.1.12(vi)]
that
\tilde{B}/\mathfrak{m}_{A}\tilde{B}=\mathcal{O}_{w}
is a valuation ring, which is
unramified over
\mathcal{O}_{v}
by
[
GR03
, Lemma 6.2.5]
.
In particular,
\mathcal{O}_{w}
is the integral closure of
\mathcal{O}_{v}
in
L
and hence

L=K\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}\mathcal{O}_{w}=A\otimes_{\tilde{A}}\tilde{B}/\mathfrak{m}_{A}\tilde{B}
. It follows that
B=A\otimes_{\tilde{A}}\tilde{B}

is a local ring with maximal ideal
A\otimes_{\tilde{A}}\mathfrak{m}_{A}\tilde{B}=\mathfrak{m}_{A}B
, where we use for
the last equality that
A=\tilde{A}_{\mathfrak{m}_{A}}
is a localization of
\tilde{A}
. On the other hand
as
\tilde{B}
is flat over
\tilde{A}
and
\mathfrak{m}_{A}
is an ideal of
\tilde{A}
we have

\mathfrak{m}_{A}\otimes_{\tilde{A}}\tilde{B}=\mathfrak{m}_{A}\tilde{B}
. Thus
\mathfrak{m}_{A}B=\mathfrak{m}_{A}\tilde{B}
.
The commutative diagram with exact rows

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{m}_{A}\tilde{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tilde{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tilde{B}/\mathfrak{m}_{A}\tilde{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{m}_{A}B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\times_{L}\mathcal{O}_{w}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{w}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

therefore yields
\tilde{B}=B\times_{L}\mathcal{O}_{w}
.

As already noted in
9.1
, the ring
\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{B})

is an étale
\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{A})
algebra. It is also faithfully flat if we show that

\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{B})\to\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{A})
is surjective. Let
p={\rm char}(\mathcal{O}_{v}/\mathfrak{m}_{v})
.
Then
\tilde{A}
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
-algebra and hence it suffices to show that

\operatorname{Spec}W_{n}(\tilde{B})\to\operatorname{Spec}W_{n}(\tilde{A})
is surjective for all
n\geq 1
, e.g.,

[
Hes15
, Proposition 1.10]
.
By
[
Bor11
, 8.1 Proposition]
there is an
integral ring map
W_{n}(\tilde{A})\to W_{n-1}(\tilde{A})\times\tilde{A}
with nilpotent kernel
and which is functorial in
\tilde{A}
. Thus the induced map

\operatorname{Spec}(W_{n-1}(\tilde{A})\times\tilde{A})\to\operatorname{Spec}\,W_{n}(\tilde{A})
is surjective,
and similarly with
\tilde{B}
instead of
\tilde{A}
.
As
\operatorname{Spec}(W_{n-1}(\tilde{B})\times\tilde{B})\to\operatorname{Spec}(W_{n-1}(\tilde{A})\times\tilde{A})
is surjective by induction,
we see that
\operatorname{Spec}W_{n}(\tilde{B})\to\operatorname{Spec}W_{n}(\tilde{A})
is surjective as well.

Finally, we show that (
9.4.1
) is an isomorphism.
Consider the composition

\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{B})\otimes_{\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{A})}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)\to\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{B})\otimes_{\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{A})}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{A}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathbb{W}_{S}(B)\otimes_{\mathbb{W}_{S}(A)}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{A}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{B}

where the first map is injective as
\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{B})
is flat over
\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{A})
,
the second map is an isomorphism as

\mathbb{W}_{S}(B)=\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{B}\otimes_{\tilde{A}}A)=\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{B})\otimes_{\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{A})}\mathbb{W}_{S}(A)
,
see
[
Bor11
, 9.4 Corollary]
, and the last map is an isomorphism by étale base change,
see
[
Hes15
, Theorem C]
. Hence (
9.4.1
) is injective
and by étale base change for
\tilde{B}/\tilde{A}
it remains to show that the elements

{\operatorname{dlog}}[b]
, for
b\in B^{\times}
, are in the image of (
9.4.1
) for
q=1
.
Let
\bar{b}
be the image of
b
in
\mathcal{O}_{w}
. As
\mathcal{O}_{w}/\mathcal{O}_{V}
is unramified
we find
a\in A^{\times}
and
u\in\tilde{B}^{\times}
such that
\bar{b}=\bar{a}\bar{u}
.
Thus
b=au(1+c)
, for some
c\in\mathfrak{m}_{B}
. Hence

{\operatorname{dlog}}[b]={\operatorname{dlog}}[a]+{\operatorname{dlog}}[u(1+c)].

Clearly the first summand lies in the image of (
9.4.1
) and
as
u(1+c)\in\tilde{B}^{\times}
the second summand lies in
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{B}}

and therefore also in the image of (
9.4.1
) by étale base change for

\tilde{B}/\tilde{A}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Definition 9.5
.

Let
X\hookrightarrow\tilde{X}
be a quasi-compact open immersion between qcqs schemes.
We denote by
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q,t}
the tame sheaf on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
associated by
8.1

and Proposition
8.2
to

\beta=\{\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}^{h}_{\tilde{X},L,w}}(\log)\subset\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{X,L}^{h}}\}_{(L,w,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Val}^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathcal{X}}}.

Considering only the tame extensions over the generic points of
X
and discrete valuations yields the tame sheaf

\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q,div}
, see
8.1
(3)
.
Note
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q,t}\subset\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q,div}
.

If
S=\{1\}
, these sheaves are equal to the sheaves
\Omega^{q,t}
and
\Omega^{q,div}

defined in
8.4
(4)

and if
S=\{1,p,\ldots,p^{n-1}\}
these sheaves are equal to the sheaves
W_{n}\Omega^{q,t}
and
W_{n}\Omega^{q,div}
defined
in
8.4
(5)
.
By the above
\mathbb{W}_{-}\Omega^{\bullet,t}(U,\tilde{U})
and
\mathbb{W}_{-}\Omega^{\bullet,div}(U,\tilde{U})
are Witt complexes over

\mathcal{O}(\tilde{U})
, in the sense of
[
Hes15
, Definition 4.1]
, for all
(U,\tilde{U})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
.

We aim to give a more explicit description of
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q,t}
and
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q,div}

in various situations of geometric and arithmetic interest.
This requires some preliminary work.

Lemma 9.6
.

In the situation of
9.3
, let
J^{q}_{S}
be the kernel of

\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{A}\to\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{K}
.
Then the diagram with vertical maps the natural inclusions

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{J^{q}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{J^{q}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}(\log)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

is commutative with exact rows and cartesian square on the right.
Moreover,

(9.6.1)

J^{q}_{S}=\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)+d(\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q-1}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)).

Proof.

For the first statement it suffices to show
J_{S}^{q}\subset\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)
.
Thus it remains to show (
9.6.1
).
This ”
\supset
” inclusion follows immediately from Lemma
9.2
.
As the right hand side in (
9.6.1
) is closed under
d
(by the Leibniz rule and
the formula
dda={\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]da
) it remains by Lemma
9.2
to show

(9.6.2)

\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{A}\subset\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)+d(\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q-1}_{\tilde{A}}(\log))=:I^{q}_{S}.

We prove this by induction over
q
, the case
q=0
being trivial.
For
q=1
, we prove for later use the stronger statement

(9.6.3)

\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{1}_{A}\subset\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)+d\mathbb{W}^{0}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})=:H^{1}_{S}\subset I^{1}_{S},

where
\mathbb{W}_{S}^{0}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})=\mathrm{ker}(\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\to\mathfrak{m}_{A})
is the kernel of the restriction map.
It suffices to show that any element of the form
V_{m}([a])dV_{n}([b])
with
a\in\mathfrak{m}_{A}
,
b\in A
,
and
n,m\in S
, lies in
H^{1}_{S}
.
As
\mathfrak{m}_{A}\subset\tilde{A}
, we may assume
b\in A\setminus\mathfrak{m}_{A}=A^{\times}
. We have

\displaystyle V_{m}([a])dV_{n}([b])

\displaystyle=V_{m}([a]F_{m}dV_{n}([b]))

\displaystyle=iV_{m}([a]dV_{n_{1}}([b]^{m_{1}}))+jV_{m}([a]V_{n_{1}}([b]^{m_{1}-1}d[b]))

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+(c-1)V_{m}([a]V_{n_{1}}([b]^{m_{1}}){\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]),

where
c={\rm gcd}(m,n)
,
m=cm_{1}
,
n=cn_{1}
, and
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfy
im+nj=c
.
Here the second equality follows from the second equality in (
9.2.3
) (with
a
there replaced by
b
here).
The third summand

(c-1)V_{m}([a]V_{n_{1}}([b]^{m_{1}}){\operatorname{dlog}}[-1])=(c-1)V_{mn_{1}}([a^{n_{1}}b^{m_{1}}]){\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]

clearly is contained in
H^{1}_{S}
, as is the second summand

jV_{m}([a]V_{n_{1}}([b]^{m_{1}-1}d[b]))=jV_{mn_{1}}([a^{n_{1}}b^{m_{1}}]){\operatorname{dlog}}[b].

If
n_{1}=1
, then we can take
j=1
and
i=0
and hence when considering the first summand we can assume
n_{1}\geq 2
.
Using the Leibniz rule and
V_{m}d=mdV_{m}
we can rewrite the first summand as

iV_{m}([a]dV_{n_{1}}([b]^{m_{1}}))=imdV_{mn_{1}}([a^{n_{1}}b^{m_{1}}])-iV_{m}(V_{n_{1}}([b]^{m_{1}})d[a])

and it remains to show that
V_{m}(V_{n_{1}}([b]^{m_{1}})d[a])
(
n_{1}\geq 2
) is contained in
H^{1}_{S}
.
We can write

[a]=[a+1]-[1]+V_{r}(\alpha),

for some
r>1
in
S
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{W}_{S/r}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})
.
(Note that
\alpha
lies in
\mathbb{W}_{S/r}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})
as
[a]+[1]-[a+1]
maps to zero in
\mathbb{W}_{S}(A/\mathfrak{m}_{A})
.)
Thus

(9.6.4)

\displaystyle V_{m}(V_{n_{1}}([b]^{m_{1}})d[a])

\displaystyle=V_{mn_{1}}([b]^{m_{1}}[a]^{n_{1}-1}d[a])

\displaystyle=V_{mn_{1}}([b]^{m_{1}}[a]^{n_{1}-1}[a+1]){\operatorname{dlog}}[a+1]+V_{mn_{1}}([b]^{m_{1}}[a]^{n_{1}-1}dV_{r}(\alpha)).

As
n_{1}\geq 2
, the first summand in this last equality is contained in
H^{1}_{S}
. Using

dV_{r}(\alpha)=F_{mn_{1}}dV_{mn_{1}r}(\alpha)-(mn_{1}-1){\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]V_{r}(\alpha),

see
[
Hes15
, Definition 4.1(iv)]
, we rewrite the second summand of the last equality in (
9.6.4
) as follows

V_{mn_{1}}([b]^{m_{1}}[a]^{n_{1}-1}dV_{r}(\alpha))\\
=V_{mn_{1}}([b^{m_{1}}a^{n_{1}-1}])dV_{mn_{1}r}(\alpha)-(mn_{1}-1)V_{mn_{1}r}\left([b^{m_{1}r}a^{(n_{1}-1)r}]\alpha\right){\operatorname{dlog}}[-1],

which hence lies in
H^{1}_{S}
as well.
This completes the proof of claim (
9.6.3
).

Now we show (
9.6.2
) for
q\geq 1
, i.e.,
for
a\in\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m})
and
\beta\in\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{A}

a\beta\in I^{q}_{S}.

We may assume that
\beta=\beta_{1}\beta_{q-1}
with
\beta_{1}\in\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{1}_{A}
and

\beta_{q-1}=db_{1}\cdots db_{q-1}
,
b_{i}\in\mathbb{W}_{S}(A)
.
By (
9.6.3
) we find
a_{i},a_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})
,
and
\gamma_{i}\in\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)
such that

a\beta=a\beta_{1}\beta_{q-1}=\sum_{i}a_{i}^{\prime}\gamma_{i}\beta_{q-1}+\sum_{i}d(a_{i})\beta_{q-1}.

Thus by induction it remains to show
dadb_{1}\cdots db_{q-1}\in I^{q}_{S}
, for
a\in\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})
.
By the Leibniz rule and the formula
ddx={\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]dx
we find an element

\gamma\in\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q-1}_{A}
such that

dadb_{1}\cdots db_{q-1}=d\left(adb_{1}\cdots db_{q-1}\right)+a\gamma{\operatorname{dlog}}[-1],

which lies in
I^{q}_{S}
by induction. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 9.7
.

Let
(L,w)/(K,v)
be a finite tame Galois extension of strictly henselian valuation fields.
Then we have the following equality of
\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathcal{O}_{w})
-submodules of
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{L}

(9.7.1)

\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}(\log)=\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathcal{O}_{w})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}(\log)+d(\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathcal{O}_{w}))\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q-1}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}(\log).

Proof.

First note that by étale base change
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{K}\to\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{L}
is injective.
Hence both sides of the statement are contained in
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{L}
and clearly the right-hand side is contained in the left-hand side.
The statement is trivially true for
q=0
.

We consider the case
q=1
.
Let
p
be the characteristic exponent of the residue field
\mathcal{O}_{v}/\mathfrak{m}_{v}
.
As
(L,w)/(K,v)
is a tame extension,
[
GR03
, Corollary 6.2.14]
yields:

L=K[b_{1},\ldots,b_{k}]
with
b_{i}^{r_{i}}=a_{i}\in K^{\times}
,
r_{i}\geq 1
and
(p,r_{i})=1
, and there is an isomorphism
of the quotient of the valuation groups
\Gamma_{L}/\Gamma_{K}
onto
\mathbb{Z}/r_{1}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathbb{Z}/r_{k}\mathbb{Z}
,
which sends
w(b_{i})
to
(0,\ldots,0,1,0,\ldots,0)
with 1 sitting at position
i
.
Up to replacing
b_{i}
and
a_{i}
by their inverse we can assume
b_{i}\in\mathcal{O}_{w}
and
a_{i}\in\mathcal{O}_{v}
.
Set
\Lambda:=\prod_{j=1}^{k}\{0,1,\ldots,r_{j}\}
.
We can write any
z\in L
as
z=\sum_{I}x_{I}b^{I}
, where
I=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k})\in\Lambda
,

x_{I}\in K
, and
b^{I}=b_{1}^{i_{1}}\cdots b_{k}^{i_{k}}
.
If
z\in\mathcal{O}_{w}
, then
c_{I}:=x_{I}b^{I}\in\mathcal{O}_{w}
, for all
I
.
Indeed, we have
w(c_{I})=v(x_{I})+\sum_{j}i_{j}w(b_{j})
in
\Gamma_{L}
which maps to the residue class of
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k})
under

\Gamma_{L}\to\Gamma_{L}/\Gamma_{K}\cong\bigoplus_{j}\mathbb{Z}/r_{j}\mathbb{Z}
. Hence all
w(c_{I})
, for
I
as above,
are pairwise different and therefore
0\leq w(z)=\min_{I}\{w(c_{I})\}
.
Thus any element in
\mathcal{O}_{w}
can be written as a sum of elements of the form

xb^{I}
, with
x\in K
and
I\in\Lambda
such that
xb^{I}\in\mathcal{O}_{w}
.
By induction over the length of
S
we see that any element in
\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathcal{O}_{w})
can be written
as a sum of elements
V_{n}([xb^{I}])
with
xb^{I}
as above.

Hence to prove the statement in the case
q=1
it suffices to show
that the elements

(1)

V_{m}([xb^{I}])dV_{n}([yb^{J}])
, for
x,y\in K^{\times}
,
I,J\in\Lambda
with
xb^{I},yb^{J}\in\mathcal{O}_{w}
,
n,m\in S
, and

(2)

\alpha\cdot{\operatorname{dlog}}[c]
, for
c\in L^{\times}
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathcal{O}_{w})
,

are contained in

(9.7.2)

\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathcal{O}_{w})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}(\log)+d\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathcal{O}_{w}).

We start by considering the elements of type
(1)
.
Set
\alpha=xb^{I}
,
\beta=yb^{J}
and
e={\rm gcd}(m,n)
and write
m=em_{1}
,
n=en_{1}
, and let
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}
with
im+jn=e
.
We have

V_{m}([\alpha])dV_{n}([\beta])=V_{m}([\alpha]F_{m}dV_{n}([\beta])).

Using the second equality in (
9.2.3
) to rewrite
F_{m}dV_{n}([\beta])
and
using further the relations
V_{m}d=mdV_{m}
,
F_{n}(d[a])=[a]^{n}{\operatorname{dlog}}[a]
,
V_{n}(a)b=V_{n}(aF_{n}(b))
and the Leibniz rule we find that the above element is equal to the following element in
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{1}_{L}

imdV_{mn_{1}}([\gamma])-iV_{mn_{1}}([\gamma]){\operatorname{dlog}}[\alpha]+jV_{mn_{1}}([\gamma]){\operatorname{dlog}}[\beta]+(e-1)V_{mn_{1}}([\gamma]){\operatorname{dlog}}[-1],

where
\gamma=\alpha^{n_{1}}\beta^{m_{1}}\in\mathcal{O}_{w}
. As
r_{j}
is invertible in
K
we have moreover

{\operatorname{dlog}}[\alpha]={\operatorname{dlog}}[x]+\sum_{j=1}^{k}i_{j}{\operatorname{dlog}}[b_{j}]={\operatorname{dlog}}[x]+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{i_{j}}{r_{j}}{\operatorname{dlog}}[a_{j}]\in\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}(\log)

and similarly with
{\operatorname{dlog}}[\beta]
. Thus the elements of type
(1)

are contained in (
9.7.2
).
Let
\alpha\cdot{\operatorname{dlog}}[c]
be an element of type
(2)
.
Let
r=r_{1}\cdots r_{k}
, which is invertible in
K
. Then
w(c^{r})
is mapped to zero in
\Gamma_{L}/\Gamma_{K}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{Z}/r_{j}\mathbb{Z}

and thus we find a unit
u\in\mathcal{O}_{w}^{\times}
and an element
x\in K^{\times}
with
c^{r}=ux
. Thus

(9.7.3)

\alpha\cdot{\operatorname{dlog}}[c]=\tfrac{1}{r}\alpha\cdot{\operatorname{dlog}}[u]+\tfrac{1}{r}\alpha\cdot{\operatorname{dlog}}[x].

Here the second summand clearly is contained in (
9.7.2
) and
the first summand is contained in
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}
, hence is a sum of elements of type
(1)

and therefore lies in (
9.7.2
) as well. This completes the proof in the case
q=1
.

For the case
q\geq 1
, we denote by
R^{q}_{S}
the right hand side of (
9.7.1
).
Note
R^{q}_{S}\subset\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}(\log)
and
dR^{q-1}_{S}\subset R^{q}_{S}
, as follows
from the Leibniz rule and the formula
ddx={\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]dx
.
From this latter formula and (
9.3.1
) it also follows that any element in

\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q-1}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}(\log)
, which is a product of elements of the form
db
, with
b\in\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathcal{O}_{w})
,
and
{\operatorname{dlog}}[c]
, with
c\in L^{\times}
, can be written as

d(\beta)+\beta^{\prime}{\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]
with
\beta,\beta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q-2}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}(\log)
.
Thus any element in
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}(\log)
is a sum of elements

\alpha d\beta\quad\text{and}\quad\alpha\beta^{\prime}{\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]\quad\text{with }\alpha\in\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}(\log),\beta,\beta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q-2}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}(\log).

We show by induction over
q
that these elements are in
R^{q}_{S}
.
By induction we have
\alpha\beta^{\prime}\in R_{S}^{q-1}
, hence
\alpha\beta^{\prime}{\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]\in R^{q}_{S}
.
By the Leibniz rule
\alpha d\beta=-d(\alpha\beta)+d(\alpha)\beta
and as
d(\alpha\beta)\in dR^{q-1}_{S}
by induction, we are
left to consider the element
d(\alpha)\beta
.
By the case
q=1
we know that
\alpha
is contained in (
9.7.2
) and hence is
a sum of elements
b\gamma+db^{\prime}
, with
b,b^{\prime}\in\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathcal{O}_{w})
and
\gamma\in\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}(\log)
.
Thus
d(\alpha)\beta
is a sum of elements

d(\gamma)b\beta-\gamma d(b)\beta+{\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]d(b^{\prime})\beta.

By induction
b\beta\in R_{S}^{q-2}
and
d(b)\beta
,
d(b^{\prime})\beta\in R_{S}^{q-1}
. Hence
d(\alpha)\beta
is contained in
R^{q}_{S}
.
∎

For
S=\{1\}
we get a stronger statement:

Lemma 9.8
.

Let
(L,w)/(K,v)
be as in Lemma
9.7
above.
Then

(9.8.1)

\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}(\log)=\mathcal{O}_{w}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}(\log).

Proof.

As
\mathcal{O}_{w}/\mathcal{O}_{v}
is flat, by
[
Bou98
, VI, §3, no. 6, Lemme 1]
, the map

\mathcal{O}_{w}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}(\log)\to\mathcal{O}_{w}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}\Omega^{q}_{K}=L\otimes_{K}\Omega^{q}_{K}=\Omega^{q}_{L}

is injective, hence it suffices to show
\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}(\log)\subset\mathcal{O}_{w}\cdot\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}(\log)
.
To this end we show
db
,
{\operatorname{dlog}}\,c\in\mathcal{O}_{w}\cdot\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}(\log)
, for all
b\in\mathcal{O}_{w}
and
c\in L^{\times}
.
Concerning the elements of type
db
note that as explained in the beginning of the proof of Lemma
9.7

(and with the notation from there) it suffices to consider elements
b=xb_{1}^{i_{1}}\cdots b_{k}^{i_{k}}\in\mathcal{O}_{w}
, with

x\in K
and
\sqrt[r_{i}]{b_{i}}=a_{i}\in K^{\times}
. In this case

db=b\cdot{\operatorname{dlog}}\,b=b\cdot{\operatorname{dlog}}\,x+\sum_{j}\tfrac{i_{j}}{r_{j}}b\cdot{\operatorname{dlog}}\,a_{j}

clearly lies in
\mathcal{O}_{w}\cdot\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}(\log)
. For
{\operatorname{dlog}}\,c
the argument around (
9.7.3
) applies.
∎

Proposition 9.9
.

Let
\tilde{A}=A\times_{K}\mathcal{O}_{v}
be as in
9.3
and assume additionally that
\mathcal{O}_{v}
is strictly henselian.
Let
A\to B
be a finite étale map of henselian local rings associated to the residue field extension
L/K
,
which we assume to be Galois and tame with respect to
v
. Let
w
be the extension of
v
to
L
and
\mathcal{O}_{w}
its valuation ring
and set
\tilde{B}=B\times_{L}\mathcal{O}_{w}
.
Then we have the following equality of
\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{B})
-submodules of
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{B}

\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{B}}(\log)=\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{B})\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)+d(\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{B}))\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q-1}_{\tilde{A}}(\log).

Proof.

Denote by
R^{q}_{S}
the right-hand side of the equality in the statement.
The image of
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{B}}(\log)
under the natural map
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{B}\to\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{L}

is by definition equal to
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}(\log)
, which by
Lemma
9.7
and our assumption is also equal to the image of
R^{q}_{S}
in
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{L}
.
By Lemma
9.6
it suffices to show that the group

\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{B})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{B}+d(\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{B})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q-1}_{B})

is contained in
R^{q}_{S}
, where
\mathfrak{m}_{B}=\mathfrak{m}_{A}B
is the maximal ideal of
B
.
As
R^{q}_{S}
is closed under the differential
d

it suffices to show

(9.9.1)

\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{B})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{B}\subset R^{q}_{S}.

As we have a surjection
\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A}\otimes_{A}B)\to\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{B})
,
[
Bor11
, Corollary 9.4]
yields

(9.9.2)

\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{B})=\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}(B).

By étale base change
[
Hes15
, Theorem C]

\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{B}=\mathbb{W}_{S}(B)\otimes_{\mathbb{W}_{S}(A)}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{A}.

Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{B})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{B}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{W}_{S}(B)\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{A}

\displaystyle\subset\mathbb{W}_{S}(B)\cdot\left(\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)+d(\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q-1}_{\tilde{A}}(\log))\right)

\displaystyle\subset\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{B})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)+\mathbb{W}_{S}(B)\cdot d(\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q-1}_{\tilde{A}}(\log))

\displaystyle\subset R^{q}_{S}+\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\cdot d(\mathbb{W}_{S}(B))\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q-1}_{\tilde{A}}(\log),

where we use (
9.9.2
) for the equality, (
9.6.1
) for the first inclusion,
(
9.9.2
) for the second inclusion, and an application of the Leibniz rule together with (
9.9.2
)
for the last inclusion.
It remains to show

(9.9.3)

\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{1}_{B}\subset R^{1}_{S}.

We prove this by induction over the cardinality of
S
.
For
S=\{1\}
this amounts by étale base change
to show that for
a\in\mathfrak{m}_{A}
,
a_{0}\in A
and
b\in B
the element

\alpha:=abda_{0}
is in
\mathfrak{m}_{B}\cdot\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)
. But this is immediate if
a_{0}\in\mathfrak{m}_{A}\subset\tilde{A}

and if
a_{0}\in A^{\times}
, this follows from
\alpha=aba_{0}{\operatorname{dlog}}a_{0}
.
Now we assume (
9.9.3
) is proven for all truncation sets of smaller cardinality
than
S
