. First observe that
V_{n}:\mathbb{W}_{S/n}\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{B}}(\log)\to\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{B}}(\log)
,
n\in S
,
induces a map

V_{n}:R^{1}_{S/n}\to R^{1}_{S}.

Indeed, any element in
R^{1}_{S/n}
can be written as sum of elements

b_{0}da_{0}+b_{1}{\operatorname{dlog}}[a_{1}]+db_{2}\\
=b_{0}F_{n}dV_{n}(a_{0})-(n-1)b_{0}a_{0}F_{n}({\operatorname{dlog}}[-1])+b_{1}F_{n}({\operatorname{dlog}}[a_{1}])+db_{2},

where
b_{i}\in\mathbb{W}_{S/n}(\tilde{B})
,
a_{0}\in\mathbb{W}_{S/n}(\tilde{A})
,
a_{1}\in A^{\times}
,
see
[
Hes15
, Definition 4.1, (iv), (v)]
for the equality.
By
[
Hes15
, Definition 4.1 (iii) and Lemma 4.3]
this is under
V_{n}

mapped to

V_{n}(b_{0})dV_{n}(a_{0})-(n-1)V_{n}(b_{0}a_{0}){\operatorname{dlog}}[-1]+V_{n}(b_{1}){\operatorname{dlog}}[a_{1}]+ndV_{n}(b_{2}),

which lies in
R^{1}_{S}
.
We show the inclusion (
9.9.3
).
By étale base change, (
9.6.3
), and (
9.9.2
) it remains to show

\mathbb{W}_{S}(B)\cdot d\mathbb{W}^{0}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\subset R^{1}_{S},

where
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})=\operatorname{Ker}(\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\to\mathfrak{m}_{A})
.
An element in the left hand side can be written as a sum of elements

bdV_{n}(a)=dV_{n}(F_{n}(b)a)-V_{n}(aF_{n}db),

for
b\in\mathbb{W}_{S}(B)
,
a\in\mathbb{W}_{S/n}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})
, and
1<n\in S
.
As
F_{n}(b)a\in\mathbb{W}_{S/n}(\mathfrak{m}_{B})
, we find
dV_{n}(F_{n}(b)a)\in R^{1}_{S}
.
By induction
aF_{n}db\in\mathbb{W}_{S/n}(\mathfrak{m}_{A})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{S/n}\Omega^{1}_{B}\subset R^{1}_{S/n}
, and
hence
V_{n}(aF_{n}db)\in R^{1}_{S}
. This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

For
S=\{1\}
we get the stronger statement:

Corollary 9.10
.

Let
\tilde{B}/\tilde{A}
be as in Proposition
9.9
above.
Then

\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{B}}(\log)=\tilde{B}\cdot\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log).

If, additionally
A
is noetherian, then we also have

\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{B}}(\log)=\tilde{B}\otimes_{\tilde{A}}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log).

Proof.

In case
A
is noetherian the ring
\tilde{B}
is a flat
\tilde{A}
-module as follows from
the argument in
[
Hüb21
, Proposition 11.8]

and the fact that in the situation at hand we have
B=\tilde{B}\otimes_{\tilde{A}}A
and

\tilde{B}
is
\tilde{A}
-torsion free. In the same way as in the beginning of the
proof of Lemma
9.8
we see that the natural maps

\tilde{B}\otimes_{\tilde{A}}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)\to\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{B}}(\log)
is injective.
Thus it remains to show the first statement in which we allow
A
also to be non-noetherian.

By Proposition
9.9
it suffices to show

d(\tilde{B})\subset\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{B}}(\log)\subset\tilde{B}\cdot\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)
.
By Lemmas
9.8
and
9.6
and since
\mathfrak{m}_{B}=\mathfrak{m}_{A}\cdot B
it remains to show that the group

\mathfrak{m}_{A}\cdot\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{B}}(\log)+d(\mathfrak{m}_{A}\cdot\tilde{B})=\mathfrak{m}_{A}\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{B}}(\log)+\tilde{B}\cdot d\mathfrak{m}_{A}

is contained in
\tilde{B}\cdot\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)
. This is clear for
\tilde{B}\cdot d\mathfrak{m}_{A}
.
By étale base change for
\Omega^{1}_{B}
any element in
\mathfrak{m}_{A}\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{B}}(\log)
is a sum of elements of the form
abda_{0}
with
a\in\mathfrak{m}_{A}
,
b\in B
,
a_{0}\in A
.
If
a_{0}\in\mathfrak{m}_{A}
, then
abda_{0}\in\tilde{B}\cdot\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)
;
if
a_{0}\in A^{\times}
, then
abda_{0}=aba_{0}{\operatorname{dlog}}\,a_{0}\in\tilde{B}\cdot\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)
.
Hence the statement.
∎

9.11
.

Let
A\to B
be a finite étale map between henselian local rings corresponding to a finite Galois extension
L/K

of the residue fields. Assume
K
is a strictly henselian valuation field with valuation
v
and valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{v}

and that
L
is tame over
K
. Denote by
w
the unique extension of
v
to
L
and by
\mathcal{O}_{w}
its valuation ring.
By
[
Bou98
, VI, §8, No. 6, Proposition 6]
the ring
\mathcal{O}_{w}
is also the integral closure of
\mathcal{O}_{v}
in
L
.
Hence the Galois group
{\rm Gal}(L/K)
is equal to the decomposition group
{\rm Aut}(\mathcal{O}_{w}/\mathcal{O}_{v})
.
Moreover, as the category of finite separable field extensions of
K
is equivalent to the category of finite local étale
A
-algebras,
we can identify the
A
-algebra automorphisms of
B
with
{\rm Gal}(L/K)
.
Hence
{\rm Gal}(L/K)={\rm Aut}(B/A)={\rm Aut}(\tilde{B}/\tilde{A})
and
\tilde{B}^{{\rm Gal}(L/K)}=\tilde{A}
.
We denote the ring map
\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{B})\to\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{B})
induced by
\sigma\in{\rm Gal}(L/K)

again by
\sigma
. Hence the usual trace map

\operatorname{Tr}:\mathbb{W}_{S}(B)\to\mathbb{W}_{S}(A),\quad b\to\sum_{\sigma\in{\rm Gal}(L/K)}\sigma(b)

restricts to

\operatorname{Tr}:\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{B})\to\mathbb{W}_{S}(\tilde{A}).

Thus the trace

\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{B}\cong\mathbb{W}_{S}(B)\otimes_{\mathbb{W}_{S}(A)}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{A}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Tr}\otimes{\operatorname{Id}}}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{A},

where the first isomorphism is étale base change, restricts by
Proposition
9.9
to a trace

\operatorname{Tr}:\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{B}}(\log)\to\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log),

given by
\beta\mapsto\sum_{\sigma}\sigma(\beta)
.

Proposition 9.12
.

Let
X\subset\tilde{X}
be a quasi-compact open immersion between qcqs schemes.
Then

\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q,t}(X,\tilde{X})=\operatorname{Ker}\left(\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}(X)\to\prod_{(x,v,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Spa}(X,\tilde{X})}\frac{\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{k(x)}}{\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}(\log)}\right),

\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q,div}(X,\tilde{X})=\operatorname{Ker}\left(\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}(X)\to\prod_{(\eta,v,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Spa}(X,\tilde{X})^{div}}\frac{\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{k(\eta)}}{\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}(\log)}\right),

where the map into the products is induced by restriction along
\operatorname{Spec}k(x)\to U
,

\mathcal{O}_{v}\subset k(x)
is the valuation ring defined by
v
, and
\mathrm{Spa}(X,\tilde{X})^{div}
denotes the subset of
\mathrm{Spa}(X,\tilde{X})
consisting of
triples
(\eta,v,\varepsilon)
with
\eta
a generic point of
X
and
v
a discrete valuation.

Proof.

This ”
\supset
” inclusion follows in both cases directly from the definition and the equality

\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}^{h}_{\tilde{X},k(x),v}}(log)=\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,x}}\times_{\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{k(x)}}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}(\log),

see Lemma
9.6
.
For the other inclusion, let
a\in\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q,t}(X,\tilde{X})
(resp.
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q,div}(X,\tilde{X})
)
and take
(x,v,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Spa}(X,\tilde{X})
(resp.
\mathrm{Spa}(X,\tilde{X})^{div}
).
By Definition
8.1
and Lemma
2.1
there exists a finite tame Galois extension

(L,w)/(K=k(x),v)
, such that
a_{L}
, the pullback of
a
along
\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{X,L}^{h}\to X
,
lies in
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}^{h}_{\tilde{X},L,w}}(\log)
.
Let
L^{sh}
and
K^{sh}
be the strict henselizations, so that
L^{sh}/K^{sh}
is
a finite Galois extension of degree
r
, which is prime to the residue characteristic of
K^{sh}
.
Thus
\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,K^{sh}}\to\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,L^{sh}}
is a finite étale extension as in

9.11
and thus

a_{K^{sh}}=\operatorname{Tr}(\tfrac{1}{r}a_{L^{sh}})\in\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}^{h}_{\tilde{X},K^{sh},v}}(\log).

By Lemma
9.4
, the map

\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{\tilde{X},K,v})\to\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{\tilde{X},K^{sh},v})
is faithfully flat and we have

\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}^{h}_{\tilde{X},K^{sh},v}}(\log)=\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{\tilde{X},K^{sh},v})\otimes_{\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{\tilde{X},K,v})}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}^{h}_{\tilde{X},K,v}}(\log),

thus descent yields
a_{K}\in\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}^{h}_{\tilde{X},K,v}}(\log)
.
As this is true for all
(x,v,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Spa}(X,\tilde{X})
, the element
a
is contained in
the respective right hand sides of the statement.
∎

Proposition 9.13
.

Let
(A,\mathfrak{m}_{A})
be a local ring with residue field
K=A/\mathfrak{m}_{A}
. Let
v
be a valuation on
K

with valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{v}
and set
\tilde{A}=A\times_{K}\mathcal{O}_{v}
.
Set
(U,\tilde{U})=(\operatorname{Spec}(A),\operatorname{Spec}(\tilde{A}))
.
Then for
q\geq 0

\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q,t}(U,\tilde{U})=\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log).

Proof.

This ”
\subset
” inclusion holds by Proposition
9.12
and
Lemma
9.6
. For the other inclusion,
let
(x,v,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Spa}(U,\tilde{U})
. It corresponds to ring maps
A\to k(x)
and
\tilde{A}\to\mathcal{O}_{v}
, which induce a morphism
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)\to\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}(\log)
.
Hence this inclusion

\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)\subset\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q,t}(U,\tilde{U})
follows from
Proposition
9.12
.
∎

Example 9.14
.

Let
\tilde{C}
be an integral, regular scheme of dimension 1. Let

\sigma:C\hookrightarrow\tilde{C}
be a dense open subscheme
and denote by

j:(C,\tilde{C})_{t}\to\tilde{C}_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}
the natural morphism of sites.

(1)

Assume the image of

\tilde{C}\to\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}
is a single point. Then there is a canonical exact sequence

(9.14.1)

0\to\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{C}}\to j_{*}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q,t}\to\bigoplus_{x\in\tilde{C}\setminus C}i_{x*}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q-1}_{k(x)}\to 0,

where
i_{x}:\operatorname{Spec}k(x)\to\tilde{C}
is the closed immersion defined by
x
.

Indeed, we have an exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{C}}\to\sigma_{*}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{C}\to\bigoplus_{x\in\tilde{C}\setminus C}i_{x*}\frac{\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{k(C)}}{\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{C},x}}\to 0.

As
\tilde{C}
is defined over a field the big de Rham–Witt decomposes as a product of
p
-typical de Rham–Witt - or classical de Rham complexes and hence this sequence follows from the structure results in
[
Ill79
]
together with the fact that locally
\tilde{C}
is a limit of schemes, which are smooth over the prime field,
by Popescu’s Theorem. By Proposition
9.12
there is a similar sequence for
j_{*}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q,t}
, where the denominator on the right hand side is replaced by

\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{C},x}(\log)
. Here we note that
it suffices to take the product in the statement of this proposition over all
(\eta,v,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Spa}(C,\tilde{C})
with
\eta\in C
the generic point and such that
\varepsilon:\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{v}\to\tilde{C}
, has center in a (closed) point of
\tilde{C}\setminus C
, say
x
.
But in this case we have inclusions

\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{C},x}\subset\mathcal{O}_{v}\subset k(C)
and as
\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{C},x}
is a
discrete valuation ring of rank one, it is a maximal subring of
k(C)
and hence
\mathcal{O}_{v}=\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{C},x}
. Putting the two sequences together an application of the snake lemma gives the exact sequence
(
9.14.1
) with the right term replaced by

\oplus_{x}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{C},x}(\log)}/\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{C},x}
.
The residue map, e.g.
[
Rül07
, Definition 2.11]
, gives an isomorphism

\operatorname{Res}_{S}^{q}:\frac{\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{C},x}(\log)}}{\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{C},x}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q-1}_{k(x)}.

For this one has to identify the completion of
\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{C},x}

with
k(x)[[t]]
, which requires the choice of a local parameter and a coefficient field. It is however not hard to show that since we only consider log-poles the residue map is in fact independent of
these choices. This yields the exact sequence (
9.14.1
).

(2)

Assume
\tilde{C}\to\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}
is finite, i.e.,
\tilde{C}
is the spectrum of
the ring of integers in a number field
K
. Then for
q\geq 1
.

j_{*}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q,t}=\sigma_{*}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{C}.

Indeed, in this case
\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{K}=\mathbb{W}_{S}(K)\otimes_{\mathbb{W}_{S}(\mathbb{Q})}\mathbb{W}_{S}\Omega^{q}_{\mathbb{Q}}=0
, for
q\geq 1
and hence
the above equality follows directly from Proposition

9.12
. If
C
is étale over
\mathbb{Z}
, this vanishes
for
q\geq 2
, by
[
Hes15
, Theorem 6.1]
and étale base change.

Proposition 9.15
.

Let
T
be a normal noetherian scheme,
\tilde{X}\to T
a smooth morphism, and
X\subset\tilde{X}
a dense open, such that

\tilde{X}\setminus X
is the support of a simple normal crossing divisor
D
over
T
(i.e., all intersections
D_{i_{1}}\cap\ldots\cap D_{i_{r}}

of the irreducible components of
D
are smooth over
T
). Let
j:(X,\tilde{X})_{t}\to\tilde{X}_{\operatorname{Zar}}
be the natural morphism of sites from above. Then

j_{*}\Omega^{q,t}_{/T}=j_{*}\Omega^{q,div}_{/T}=\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{X}/T}(\log D).

Proof.

We may assume
\tilde{X}
is integral. Clearly, we have the inclusions

\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{X}/T}(\log D)\subset j_{*}\Omega^{q,t}_{/T}\subset j_{*}\Omega^{q,div}_{/T}

and the equality

\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{X}/T}(\log D)=\operatorname{Ker}\left(\Omega^{q}_{X/T}\to\bigoplus_{x\in D^{(0)}}\Omega^{q}_{\eta/T}/\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{X},x/T}(\log D)\right),

where
\eta\in\tilde{X}
is the generic point. Thus it remains to show:
Let
R
be a ring and
(K,v)
a discrete valuation field, such that
\mathcal{O}_{v}
is an
R
-algebra.
Let
\alpha\in\Omega^{q}_{K/R}
and assume there exists a finite tame Galois extension
(L,w)/(K,v)
such that

\alpha_{L}\in\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}(\log)
. Then
\alpha\in\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}/R}(\log)
.

To this end, we may assume by étale descent that
(K,v)
and
(L,w)
are strictly henselian.
We have a surjection
\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}(\log)\rightarrow\!\!\!\!\!\rightarrow\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}/R}(\log)
and hence
by Lemma
9.8

\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}/R}(\log)=\mathcal{O}_{w}\cdot\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}/R}(\log).

Hence the trace from
9.11
for
S=\{1\}
also induces a trace for relative differentials

\operatorname{Tr}:\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}/R}(\log)\to\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}/R}(\log).

Denoting by
e=[L:K]
the degree, which is prime to the residue characteristic of
\mathcal{O}_{v}
, we obtain

\alpha=\frac{1}{e}\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha_{L})\in\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}/R}(\log)
. This completes the proof.
∎

10.
Tame de Rham–Witt cohomology in positive characteristic

Throughout this section
k
is a perfect field of positive characteristic
p
,

X
is a smooth
k
-scheme of finite type and
X\hookrightarrow\tilde{X}
is a dense open immersion into
a separated and finite type
k
-scheme
\tilde{X}
. By Definition
9.5

we can consider the tame (
p
-typical) de Rham–Witt complex

W_{n}\Omega^{\bullet,t}
and the divisorial version
W_{n}\Omega^{\bullet,{div}}
on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
.

We set
\mathcal{X}=(X,\tilde{X})
and denote
by
j:(X,\tilde{X})_{t}\to\tilde{X}_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}
the morphism of sites induced by the functor

(\tilde{V}\subset\tilde{X})\mapsto(\tilde{V}\cap X,\tilde{V})
.

Proposition 10.1
.

Assume
\tilde{X}
is smooth and the complement
\tilde{X}\setminus X
is the support of a simple normal
crossing divisor
D
. Then

j_{*}W_{n}\Omega^{q,t}=j_{*}W_{n}\Omega^{q,div}=W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{X}}(\log D),

where the right hand side denotes
q
-forms of the logarithmic de Rham–Witt complex,
associated to the
smooth log scheme
(\tilde{X},j_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times}\cap\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{X}})
, see
[
HK94
]
,
[
Mat17
]
.

Proof.

For definiteness, we set

W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{X}}(\log D):=W_{n}\Lambda^{q}_{(\tilde{X},\mathcal{M})/(\operatorname{Spec}k,{\rm triv})}

with the notation from
[
Mat17
, Proposition-Definition 3.10]
,
where
\mathcal{M}=j_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times}\cap\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{X}}
is the log structure defined by
D
and we equip the base
\operatorname{Spec}k

with the trivial log structure. If
U
is a smooth
k
-scheme and
x\in U
is a 1-codimensional point such that its closure

D_{x}
in
U
is smooth, and
L
is the fraction field of
\mathcal{O}_{U,x}

(resp. the fraction field of its strict henselization at a geometric point
\bar{x}
over
x
)
and
w
is the discrete valuation defined by
x
on
L
,
then it follows from étale base change and
[
Mat17
, Corollary 4.4]

that the Zariski stalk at
x
(resp. the étale stalk at
\bar{x}
) of
W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{U}(\log D_{x})
is equal to

W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}(\log)
. Furthermore, it follows from the explanations in
[
Mat17
, 9.]
and
[
Ill79
, I, Corollaire 3.9]

that
W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{X}}(\log D)
is a successive extension of locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{X}}
-modules and hence is
Cohen–Macaulay. Thus
W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{X}}(\log D)=j_{*}W_{n}\Omega^{q,div}
, by Proposition
9.12

with
S=\{1,p,\ldots,p^{n-1}\}
. Thus also
j_{*}W_{n}\Omega^{q,t}\subset W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{X}}(\log D)
.
On the other hand it follows from the local description of
W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{X}}(\log D)
, given by
[
Mat17
, Corollary 4.4]

and étale base change, that the pullback of a local section

\alpha\in W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{X}}(\log D)
along

(\operatorname{Spec}L,\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{w})\to(X,\tilde{X})
, with
(L,w)
a valuation field,
lies in
W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{w}}(\log)
.
Hence the inclusion
W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{X}}(\log D)\subset j_{*}W_{n}\Omega^{q,t}
.
∎

10.2
.

Consider the following condition:

({\rm princ})_{d}

Assume
(\tilde{X},D)
is a normal crossing pair of dimension
d
,
i.e.,
\tilde{X}
is a smooth integral
k
-scheme of dimension
d
and
D
is a divisor on
\tilde{X}
which has simple normal crossings.
Assume further that
Z\subset\tilde{X}
is a closed (possibly non-reduced) subscheme of codimension

\geq 2
which is contained in
D
.
Then there exists a finite sequence of morphisms

\pi:\tilde{Y}=\tilde{X}_{n}\xrightarrow{\pi_{n}}\tilde{X}_{n-1}\to\ldots\to\tilde{X}_{1}\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}}\tilde{X}_{0}=\tilde{X},

such that

(a)

each
(\tilde{X}_{j},D_{j})
is a normal crossing pair, where
D_{0}:=D
and

D_{j}=\pi^{-1}_{j}(D_{j-1})_{\rm red}
,
j\geq 1
,

(b)

each
\pi_{j}
,
1\leq j\leq n
, is the blow-up in a smooth closed subscheme
C_{j-1}\subset X_{j-1}
, which has normal crossings with
D_{j-1}
,

(c)

each
C_{j}
,
0\leq j\leq n-1
is contained in
Z_{j}
, where

Z_{0}:=Z
and
Z_{j}
is the strict transform of
Z_{j-1}
under
\pi_{j}
,

(d)

\pi^{-1}Z
is an Cartier divisor on
\tilde{Y}
.

Recall that given a normal crossing pair
(\tilde{X},D)
, we say that a closed subscheme
C\subset D
has
normal crossings with
D
,
if for every point
x\in C
we find a regular parameter sequence
t_{1},\ldots,t_{r}\in\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
such that
the ideal of
C
at
x
is given by
(t_{1},\ldots,t_{b})
and
D
is at
x
given by
{\rm div}(t_{a}\cdots t_{c})

with
1\leq a\leq b\leq c\leq r
.

It is well-known that

(
princ
)

d

holds for
0\leq d\leq 2
, see e.g.

[
Sta16
,
Tag 0AHH
]
;
in characteristic zero it holds by Hironaka. Notice that

(
princ
)

d

is trivially satisfied
if
D=\emptyset
.

Theorem 10.3
.

Assume
\tilde{X}
is smooth and the complement
\tilde{X}\setminus X
is the support of a simple normal
crossing divisor
D
. Assume

(
princ
)

d

holds for
d=\dim X
.
Then

R\Gamma((X,\tilde{X})_{t},W_{n}\Omega^{q,t})=R\Gamma(\tilde{X}_{{\operatorname{Zar}}},W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{X}}(\log D)).

Moreover, setting

W\Omega^{\bullet,t}:=R\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}W_{n}\Omega^{\bullet,t},

we get

R\Gamma((X,\tilde{X})_{t},W\Omega^{\bullet,t})=R\Gamma_{\text{log-crys}}((\tilde{X},D)/W(k)),

where the right hand is logarithmic crystalline cohomology,
[
HK94
]
:

R\Gamma_{\text{log-crys}}((\tilde{X},D)/W(k))=R\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}R\Gamma_{\text{log-crys}}((\tilde{X},D)/W_{n}(k)).

Proof.

For the first statement, observe that by Proposition
10.1
we have a natural map

W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{X}}(\log D)=j_{*}W_{n}\Omega^{q,t}\to Rj_{*}W_{n}\Omega^{q,t}.

It remains to show that this yields an isomorphism after applying
H^{i}(\tilde{X}_{{\operatorname{Zar}}},-)
.
By Theorem
7.2
we have

H^{i}((X,\tilde{X})_{t},W_{n}\Omega^{q,t})=\varinjlim_{\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}}H^{i}(\tilde{Y}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}},j^{\mathcal{Y}}_{*}W_{n}\Omega^{q,t}),

where the direct limit is over all modifications
\mathcal{Y}=(X,\tilde{Y})\to\mathcal{X}
.
Under

(
princ
)

d

modifications as in

(
princ
)

d

are cofinal in the
cofiltered category of all modifications to
\mathcal{X}
,
see, e.g., the argument in the proof of

[
Sta16
,
Tag 0AHI
]
.
Using further that étale- and Zariski cohomology of
W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{X}}(\log D)
coincide, we obtain

H^{i}((X,\tilde{X})_{t},W_{n}\Omega^{q,t})=\varinjlim_{\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}}H^{i}(\tilde{Y}_{{\operatorname{Zar}}},W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{Y}}(\log D^{\prime})),

where the limit is over modifications induced by morphisms
\pi:\tilde{Y}\to\tilde{X}
as in

(
princ
)

d

and
and where
D^{\prime}:=\pi^{-1}(D)_{\rm red}
. The pullback

\pi^{*}:H^{i}(\tilde{X}_{{\operatorname{Zar}}},W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{X}}(\log D))\to H^{i}(\tilde{X}_{{\operatorname{Zar}}},W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{Y}}(\log D^{\prime}))

is an isomorphism by
[
Sai21
, Theorem 5.2]
and the fact that with the notation from

loc. cit.
the Nisnevich sheaf given by

U\mapsto\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}W_{n}\Omega^{q}(U,D_{|U})
is equal to

W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{\tilde{X}}(\log D)
.
For
q=0
, this follows from
[
RS21
, Theorem 4.15(4) and Theorem 7.20]
,
for
q\geq 1
this holds by
[
RR24
, Theorem 5.4 and Remark 5.5(3)]

or the proof of
[
Mer25
, Theorem 4.4]
. This yields the first statement.

Considering the total complex of the double complex

(\ldots\to R\Gamma((X,\tilde{X})_{t},W_{n}\Omega^{q,t})\xrightarrow{d}R\Gamma((X,\tilde{X})_{t},W_{n}\Omega^{q+1,t})\to\ldots)
and applying
R\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}

yields the second statement.
∎

Remark 10.4
.

Let
(X,\tilde{X})
be as at the beginning of this section with
X
integral of dimension
d
.
Assume

(
princ
)

d

and assume resolution of singularities hold in dimension
d
, in the sense that
for any finite type and separated
k
-scheme
Y
of dimension
d
and every reduced closed subset
Z\subset Y

such that
Y\setminus Z
is smooth, there exists a proper morphism
f:Y_{1}\to Y
which is an isomorphism
over
Y\setminus Z
, such that
Y_{1}
is smooth over
k
and
f^{-1}(Z)_{{\operatorname{red}}}
has simple normal crossings.
Then we have

(10.4.1)

R\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}W_{n}\Omega^{q,t}=\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}W_{n}\Omega^{q,t},

i.e.,
R^{i}\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}W_{n}\Omega^{q,t}=0
, for all
i\geq 1
.

Indeed by resolution of singularities every object of
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
has a covering
consisting of pairs
(V,\tilde{V})
with
\tilde{V}
smooth and affine, such that

\tilde{V}\setminus V
has simple normal crossing support. For such pairs we have

H^{i}((V,\tilde{V})_{t},W_{n}\Omega^{q,t})=0,\quad\text{for all }i>0,

by the first statement of Theorem
10.3
, which uses

(
princ
)

d

. Moreover, the restriction maps

W_{n+1}\Omega^{q,t}(V,\tilde{V})\to W_{n}\Omega^{q,t}(V,\tilde{V})
are surjective by Proposition
10.1
,
whence (
10.4.1
) holds by
[
Sta16
,
Tag 0BKY
]
.

Remark 10.5
.

We remark, that if
k=\mathbf{C}
, then

(
princ
)

d

holds for any
d
and the same proof for Theorme
10.3

together with
[
Del71
]
shows in case
\tilde{X}
is proper

R\Gamma((X,\tilde{X})_{t},\Omega^{\bullet,t}_{/\mathbf{C}})\cong R\Gamma_{\rm Betti}(X(\mathbf{C}),\mathbf{C}),

where we use Proposition
9.15
instead of Proposition
10.1
.

Definition 10.6
.

Following
[
LZ07
, after Definition 2.1]
3
3

3

See also
[
LZ07
, Proposition 4.4]
for the relation with other definitions of the Nygaard complex in the non-tame setup.

we define the
r
th
tame Nygaard complex
of level
n
on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
(
r\geq 0
) by

\mathcal{N}^{r}W_{n}\Omega^{\bullet,t}:0\to W_{n-1}\mathcal{O}^{t}\xrightarrow{d}\ldots\xrightarrow{d}W_{n-1}\Omega^{r-1,t}\xrightarrow{dV}W_{n}\Omega^{r,t}\xrightarrow{d}W_{n}\Omega^{r+1,t}\to\ldots

We obtain a pro-complex
\mathcal{N}^{r}W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{\bullet,t}
in which the transition maps
are induced by restriction. We set

\mathcal{N}^{r}W\Omega^{\bullet,t}=R\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}\mathcal{N}^{r}W_{n}\Omega^{\bullet,t}.

We have two morphism of pro-complexes

\iota_{r},F_{r}:\mathcal{N}^{r}W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}\to W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{\bullet,t},

which in degree
q
are given by

\iota_{r}^{q}=\begin{cases}p^{r-q-1}V&\text{if }q\leq r-1\\
{\rm id}&\text{if }q\geq r,\end{cases}\quad F_{r}^{q}=\begin{cases}{\rm id}&\text{if }q\leq r-1\\
p^{q-r}F&\text{if }q\geq r.\end{cases}

It is direct to check that these define morphism of complexes, cf.
[
LZ07
, (5)]
.
We define the
r
th tame syntomic pro-complex on
(X\,\tilde{X})_{t}
as

\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}(r)^{t}=\operatorname{Cone}(F_{r}-\iota_{r}:\mathcal{N}^{r}W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{\bullet,t}\to W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{\bullet,t})[-1],

and the
r
th tame syntomic complex by

\mathbb{Z}_{p}(r)^{t}=R\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}(r)^{t}.

Definition 10.7
.

Let
W_{n}\Omega^{r}_{X,\log}
4
4

4

Also denoted by
\nu_{n}(r)
or
\nu_{n}^{r}
in the literature.

be the sheaf on
X_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}
of logarithmic de Rham–Witt differentials of degree
r
,
e.g.,
[
Ill79
, I, 5.7]
. We define a presheaf
W_{n}\Omega^{r,t}_{\log}
on

(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
by

W_{n}\Omega^{r,t}_{\log}(V,\tilde{V}):=\Gamma(V,W_{n}\Omega^{r}_{X,\log}).

It follows directly from the definition, that
W_{n}\Omega^{r,t}_{\log}
coincides with

F_{\beta}
, where
F=W_{n}\Omega^{r}_{X,\log}
and

\beta=\{F_{w}\subset F(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,L})\}_{(L,w,\varepsilon\in\mathrm{Val}^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathcal{X}})}
with
F_{w}=F(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,L})

in the notation of
8.1
.
Hence
W_{n}\Omega^{r,t}_{\log}
is a sheaf on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
.

Theorem 10.8
.

We have an isomorphism of pro-complexes
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}

\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}(r)^{t}=W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{r,t}_{\log}[-r].

Proof.

This is the tame version of
[
BMS19
, Proposition 8.4]
.
Indeed,
\iota_{r}^{q}
(resp.
F^{q}_{r}
) is nilpotent in degrees
\leq r_{1}
(resp.
\geq r
) and hence

\iota_{r}-F_{r}
is an isomorphism of pro-complexes in degrees
\neq r
.
It remains to show that

{\rm id}-F:W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{r,t}\to W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{r,t}

is surjective on
(X,\tilde{X})
with kernel
W\Omega^{q,t}_{\log}
.
By Proposition
5.2
, Remark
5.6
, and Proposition
9.13

it suffices to show that the sequence

(10.8.1)

0\to W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{r}_{A,\log}\to W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{r}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)\xrightarrow{{\rm id}-F}W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{r}_{\tilde{A}}(\log)\to 0

is exact for henselian local rings
(A,\mathfrak{m})
with a valuation
v
on the residue field
K=A/\mathfrak{m}
,
such that
A
is ind-étale over
X
,
(K,v)
is tamely closed, and
\tilde{A}=A\times_{K}\mathcal{O}_{v}
.
As (
10.8.1
) is a subsequence of the sequence

0\to W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{r}_{A,\log}\to W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{r}_{A}\xrightarrow{{\rm id}-F}W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{r}_{A},

which is exact by
[
Ill79
, I, Théorème 5.7.2]
,
we get that the sequence (
10.8.1
) is
left exact as well. As
\tilde{A}
is strictly henselian, the map

W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{r}_{\tilde{A}}\xrightarrow{{\rm id}-F}W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{r}_{\tilde{A}}
is surjective,
by
loc. cit.
Thus the formula
Fd\log[x]={\operatorname{dlog}}[x]
yields
the right exactness of (
10.8.1
).
∎

Corollary 10.9
.

There is a natural cycle map

(10.9.1)

{\rm CH}^{r}(X,2r-i)\to H^{i}((X,\tilde{X})_{t},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(r)^{t})\to H^{i}((X,\tilde{X})_{t},W\Omega^{\bullet,t}

),
where the left hand side denotes Bloch’s higher Chow groups.
This map is functorial with respect to pullback along
k
-morphisms
(Y,\tilde{Y})\to(X,\tilde{X})
, with

\tilde{Y}
a finite type separated
k
-scheme and
Y\subset\tilde{Y}
a dense open which is smooth.
This map vanishes for
r>i
.

Moreover, if
\tilde{X}
is smooth and proper over
k
and
i=2r
, then this cycle map fits into a
commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\operatorname{CH}}^{r}(\tilde{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{r}_{\rm crys}(\tilde{X}/W(k))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{CH}}^{r}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{r}((X,\tilde{X})_{t},W\Omega^{\bullet,t}),}

where the top horizontal map is the classical crystalline cycle map,
the left vertical map is restriction along the open immersion
X\hookrightarrow\tilde{X}
, and
the right vertical map is induced from
the identification
H^{r}_{\rm crys}(X/W(k))=H^{r}((\tilde{X},\tilde{X})_{t},W\Omega^{\bullet,t})
stemming from
Theorem
10.3
and pullback along
(\tilde{X},\tilde{X})\to(X,\tilde{X})
.

Proof.

We denote by
\mathbb{Z}(r)_{X}
Bloch’s complex of Zariski sheaves
U\mapsto z^{r}(U,*)[-2r]

[
Blo86
]
, which is an avatar of the motivic complex of weight
r
.
Let
i:Y=\tilde{X}\setminus X\hookrightarrow\tilde{X}
be the closed immersion,
and denote by
\sigma:X\hookrightarrow\tilde{X}
the open immersion and by
c={\rm codim(Y,\tilde{X})}
the codimension.
Consider the following composition of maps between pro-complexes

\frac{\mathbb{Z}(r)_{\tilde{X}}}{i_{*}\mathbb{Z}(r)_{Y}}\to\sigma_{*}\mathbb{Z}(r)_{X}\to\sigma_{*}\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}(r)_{X}\cong\sigma_{*}W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{r}_{X,\log}[-r]=j_{*}W_{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\Omega^{r,t}_{\log}[-r]\to Rj_{*}\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}(r)^{t},

where the isomorphism in the middle is
[
GL00
, Theorem 8.3]
, the equality holds by definition of

W_{n}\Omega^{q,t}_{\log}
, and the last map is induced by Theorem
10.8
.
Applying
R\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}R\Gamma(\tilde{X},-)
and using

R\Gamma(\tilde{X}_{{\operatorname{Zar}}},\frac{\mathbb{Z}(r)_{\tilde{X}}}{i_{*}\mathbb{Z}(r)_{Y}})\cong\Gamma(X,\mathbb{Z}(r)_{X})
,
which holds by
[
Blo86
, Theorems (3.3) and (3.4)]
together with
[
Blo94
]
,
we get the first map in (
10.9.1
).
The second map is the composition

H^{i}((X,\tilde{X})_{t},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(r)^{t})\to H^{i}((X,\tilde{X}),R\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}\mathcal{N}^{r}W_{n}\Omega^{\bullet,t})\ \xrightarrow{\iota_{r}}H^{i}((X,\tilde{X}),R\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}W_{n}\Omega^{\bullet,t}).

This completes the construction of the cycle map.
The other statements follow immediately from this construction.
∎

11.
Tame sheaves associated to reciprocity sheaves

11.1
.

We explain how to apply the construction
8.1
to reciprocity sheaves.
Let
k
be a perfect field of characteristic
p\geq 0

and let
X\hookrightarrow\tilde{X}
be an open immersion between separated finite type

k
-schemes such that
\tilde{X}\setminus X
is the support of an effective Cartier divisor
and
X
is smooth over
k
. Set
\mathcal{X}=(X,\tilde{X})
.

Let
F
be a reciprocity sheaf in the sense of
[
KSY22
]
, see also
[
KSY16
]
.
In particular it is defined on
\operatorname{\mathrm{Sm}}_{k}
the category of smooth separated
k
-schemes.
We denote by
\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F
the maximal cube-invariant and semipure modulus sheaf with
transfers, which has
M
-reciprocity and extends
F
, see
[
Sai21
, (1.0.4)]
.
In the following we assume that
F
additionally defines a sheaf on
X_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
.

Let
(L,w,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Val}^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathcal{X}}
. We say a discrete valuation
v
on
L
is of
geometric type over
k
, if there is some smooth integral
k
-scheme
U
with function field

L
and some point
y\in U^{(1)}
, such that
\mathcal{O}_{v}=\mathcal{O}_{U,y}
. We say such a
discrete valuation
v
is a
generalization of
w
, if
U
and
y
can be chosen in such way
that
\operatorname{Spec}L\to X
extends to
U\to\tilde{X}
and the image of the closed point
of
\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{w}
in
\tilde{X}
is contained in the closure of the image of
y
in
\tilde{X}
. We denote by

\Phi_{w}
the set of all discrete valuations of geometric type over
k
on
L
generalizing
w
.
For
v\in\Phi_{w}
we denote by
L^{h}_{v}
and
\mathcal{O}_{v}^{h}
the henselizations.
By taking colimits over geometric models we can define

F(L^{h}_{v})\quad\text{and}\quad\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\mathcal{O}_{v}^{h},\mathfrak{m}_{v}^{-1}),

where
\mathfrak{m}_{v}
denotes the maximal ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{v}^{h}
.
In particular
\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\mathcal{O}_{v}^{h},\mathfrak{m}_{v}^{-1})
is a colimit of groups of the form

\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\tilde{V},D)
, where
\tilde{V}
is smooth and affine with a morphism
\tilde{V}\to\tilde{X}
and
D
is a smooth divisor on
\tilde{V}
.

Define
\log F(w)
to be the subgroup of
F(L)
given by

(11.1.1)

\log F(w)=\operatorname{Ker}\left(F(L)\to\prod_{v\in\Phi_{w}}\frac{F(L^{h}_{v})}{\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\mathcal{O}_{v}^{h},\mathfrak{m}_{v}^{-1})}\right)

and set

\log F_{w}:=F(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,L})\times_{F(L)}\log F(w).

We claim, that

\beta^{\rm rec}_{\log}:=\{\log F_{w}\subset F(\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,L})\}_{(L,w,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Val}^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathcal{X}}}

satisfies the assumption
(

β

1)
of
8.1
.
To this end it suffices to show that if
f:\tilde{V}_{1}\to\tilde{V}
is a morphism and
D
is a smooth divisor on
\tilde{V}
, such that

D_{1}:=f^{-1}(D)_{\rm red}
is a smooth divisor and
\tilde{V}_{1}\setminus D_{1}\to V\setminus D
is étale, then

f^{*}:F(\tilde{V}\setminus D)\to F(\tilde{V}_{1}\setminus D_{1})
induces a morphism

\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\tilde{V},D)\to\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}(\tilde{V}_{1},D_{1})
,
which is a consequence of
[
Sai21
, Theorem 4.2]
.
Thus we obtain a sheaf
F_{\beta^{\rm rec}_{\log}}
on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
.
This construction yields a functor

\mathbf{RSC}_{X_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}\to{\rm Sh}((X,\tilde{X})_{t}),\quad F\mapsto F_{\beta^{\rm rec}_{\log}},

where
\mathbf{RSC}_{X_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}}=\mathbf{RSC}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\cap{\rm Sh}(X_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}})
is the category of reciprocity sheaves which are also étale sheaves on
X
.

Lemma 11.2
.

Assume
\tilde{X}
is smooth and
\tilde{X}\setminus X
is the support of a simple normal crossing divisor
D
.
Let
F
be a reciprocity sheaf which is also a sheaf of
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
-modules on
X_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}

and assume that for all
\eta\in D^{(0)}
the natural map

(11.2.1)

\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{X},\eta}^{h},\mathfrak{m}_{\eta}^{-1})\xrightarrow{\simeq}F(K^{h}_{\eta})\times_{F(K^{sh}_{\eta})}\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{X},\eta}^{sh},\mathfrak{m}_{\eta}^{-1})

is an isomorphism, where
K_{\eta}^{(s)h}={\operatorname{Frac}}(\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{X},\eta}^{(s)h})
.

Then with the notation from
11.1

F_{\beta^{\rm rec}_{\rm log}}(X,\tilde{X})=\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\tilde{X},D).

Proof.

We start by showing this
\supset
inclusion. Let
a\in\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\tilde{X},D)\subset F(X)
.
Let
(x,w,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Spa}(X,\tilde{X})
. It suffices to show that
a_{k(x)}
the pullback of
a
to
F(k(x))
lies
in
\log F(w)
as defined in (
11.1.1
). But for
v\in\Phi_{w}
we have a natural map

\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{v}^{h}\to\tilde{X}
and it follows from
[
Sai21
, Theorem 4.2]
, that this map induces a morphism

\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\tilde{X},D)\to\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\mathcal{O}_{v}^{h},\mathfrak{m}_{v}^{-1})
. Hence
a_{k(x)}\in\log F(w)
.
Now the other inclusion. Let
a\in F_{\beta^{\rm rec}_{\log}}(X,\tilde{X})
.
By
[
Sai21
, Corollary 2.5]
it suffices to show that for each generic point
\eta
of
D
the pullback
of
a
to
F(K^{h}_{\eta})
, where
K=k(\tilde{X})
, lies in
\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{X},\eta}^{h},\mathfrak{m}_{\eta}^{-1})
.
Let
v
be the discrete valuation on
K
corresponding to
\eta
, giving rise to the point
(\eta,v,\varepsilon)\in\mathrm{Spa}(X,\tilde{X})
,
where
\varepsilon:\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{X},\eta}\to\tilde{X}
is the natural map.
By definition of
F_{\beta^{\rm rec}_{\log}}
we find a finite tame extension
(L,w)/(K,v)
such that

a_{L}\in\log F_{w}=\log F(w)=\operatorname{Ker}\left(F(L)\to\frac{F(L_{w}^{h})}{\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\mathcal{O}_{w}^{h},\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{-1})}\right),

where we use that
w
is already a discrete valuation of geometric type over
k
on
L
and hence

\Phi_{w}=\{w\}
. We have a finite morphism of pro-modulus pairs
(see
[
RS21
, Lemma 3.8]
)
f:(\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{w}^{sh},e\cdot s_{w})\to(\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{X},\eta}^{sh},\eta)
,
where
s_{w}
denotes the closed point and
e

is the ramification index, which is also equal to the degree of
f
.
The transfer structure thus induces a pushforward

f_{*}:\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\mathcal{O}_{w}^{sh},\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{-1})\subset\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\mathcal{O}_{w}^{sh},\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{-e})\to\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{X},\eta}^{sh},\mathfrak{m}_{\eta}^{-1}).

By tameness,
(e,p)=1
and hence

a_{K_{\eta}^{sh}}=\tfrac{1}{e}f_{*}(a_{L_{w}^{sh}})\in\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{X},\eta}^{sh},\mathfrak{m}_{\eta}^{-1}).

Thus
a_{K_{\eta}^{h}}\in\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{X},\eta}^{h},\mathfrak{m}_{\eta}^{-1})
,
by (
11.2.1
), which completes the proof.
∎

Theorem 11.3
.

Let
F
be a reciprocity sheaf and fix integers
i,d\geq 0
.
Assume for any normal crossing pair
(\tilde{Y},D)
with
\dim\tilde{Y}=d
,

(a)

the assignment

U/\tilde{Y}\mapsto\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(U,D_{|U})
defines an
étale sheaf of
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
-modules on
\tilde{Y}
, denoted by
F_{(\tilde{Y},D)}
,

(b)

H^{i}(\tilde{Y}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}},F_{(\tilde{Y},D)})=H^{i}(\tilde{Y}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}},F_{(\tilde{Y},D)})
.

Assume
\tilde{X}
is smooth of dimension
d
and
D=\tilde{X}\setminus X
is an SNCD.
Assume
10.2
,

(
princ
)

d

holds. Then

H^{i}((X,\tilde{X})_{t},F_{\beta_{\log}^{\rm rec}})=H^{i}(\tilde{X}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}},F_{(\tilde{X},D)}).

Proof.

Under

(
princ
)

d

the modifications as in

(
princ
)

d

are cofinal in the cofiltered category of all modifications to
\mathcal{X}
,
see, e.g., the argument in the proof of

[
Sta16
,
Tag 0AHI
]
.
Thus

(11.3.1)

H^{i}((X,\tilde{X})_{t},F_{\beta_{\log}^{\rm rec}})=\varinjlim_{\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}}H^{i}(\tilde{Y}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}},j_{*}^{\mathcal{Y}}F_{\beta_{\log}^{\rm rec}})=\varinjlim_{\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}}H^{i}(\tilde{Y}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}},F_{(\tilde{Y},D)}),

where the direct limit is over all modifications
\mathcal{Y}=(X,\tilde{Y})\to\mathcal{X}
as in

(
princ
)

d

.
The first equality holds by Theorem
7.2
,
and the second equality by assumptions
(a)
,
(b)
, and Lemma
11.2
. Thus it remains to prove the colimit on the right is constant.
Let
\pi:\tilde{Y}\to\tilde{X}
be a morphism as in

(
princ
)

d

and set
D^{\prime}:=\pi^{-1}(D)_{\rm red}
.
In this case the pullback

\pi^{*}:H^{i}(\tilde{X}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}},F_{(\tilde{X},D)})\to H^{i}(\tilde{Y}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}},F_{(\tilde{Y},D^{\prime})})

is an isomorphism by
[
Sai21
, Theorem 5.2]
, hence the statement.
∎

Example 11.4
.

The following reciprocity sheaves satisfy the assumptions
(a)
and
(b)
of Theorem
11.3
:

•

G
a smooth unipotent commutative
k
-group, as in this case
\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}G_{(\tilde{Y},D_{{\operatorname{red}}})}=G_{\tilde{Y}}
, as follows from

[
RS21
, Theorem 5.2]
;

•

\Omega^{q}_{/k}
, as follows from the description of
\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\Omega^{q}_{/k}
in
[
RS21
, Corollary 6.8]

(if
{\rm char}(k)=0
) and in
[
RS22
, Corollary 6.8]
(if
{\rm char}(k)\geq 0
);

•

W_{n}\Omega^{q}
, if
{\rm char}(k)=p>0
, as follows from
the description of
\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}W_{n}\Omega^{q}_{(\tilde{Y},D_{{\operatorname{red}}})}

in
[
Mer25
, Theorem 4.4]
or
[
RR24
, Theorem 5.4]
;

•

B_{n}\Omega^{q}=F^{n-1}dW_{n}\Omega^{q}
and
Z_{n}\Omega^{q}=F^{n}W_{n+1}\Omega^{q}
as follows from
[
RR24
, Corollary 6.16]

(still assuming
{\rm char}(k)=p>0
).

Note that by
[
CR11
, Theorem 3.2.8]
the cohomology
R\Gamma(\tilde{X},\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{X}})
is a birational invariant
and hence so is
R\Gamma(\tilde{X},W_{n}\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{X}})
(using
[
BRS22
, Proposition 9.10]
).
Thus in this case the direct limit in (
11.3.1
) for general modifications

\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
stabilizes as soon as the following condition is satisfied for
d=\dim X
:

({\rm res})_{d}

For
\tilde{Y}
an integral and separated finite type
k
-scheme of dimension
d

with a dense smooth open subscheme
Y\subset\tilde{X}
, there exists a proper morphism

f:\tilde{Z}\to\tilde{Y}
, such that its restriction
f^{-1}(Y)\xrightarrow{\simeq}Y
is an isomorphism and
f^{-1}(\tilde{Y}\setminus Y)_{\rm red}
is an SNCD.

If
{\rm char}(k)=0
, then

(
res
)

d

holds for all
d
by Hironaka;
if
{\rm char}(k)=p>0
, then

(
res
)

d

holds for
0\leq d\leq 3
.
For this first take a resolution
h:\tilde{Y}_{1}\to\tilde{Y}
,
such that
h^{-1}(Y)\xrightarrow{\simeq}Y
is an isomorphism,
see
[
CP19
, Theorem 1.1]
, then
apply
[
CJS20
, Theorem 1.4]
(with
B=\emptyset
) to get a projective morphism

g:\tilde{Y}_{2}\to\tilde{Y}
such that
g^{-1}(Y)\xrightarrow{\simeq}Y
is an isomorphism and

g^{-1}(\tilde{Y}\setminus Y)_{\rm red}=A\cup E
with
E
a simple normal crossing divisor
and
A
smooth intersecting
E
transversally, then blow-up once more in
A
to get

f:\tilde{Z}\to\tilde{Y}
as in

(
res
)

d

.

In particular for
\tilde{X}
as in Theorem
11.3
with
\dim X\leq 3
we get

R\Gamma((X,\tilde{X})_{t},W_{n}\mathcal{O}^{t})=R\Gamma(\tilde{X}_{\rm Zar},W_{n}\mathcal{O}),

a version of which was obtained in
[
Hüb21
, Theorem 13.7]
for
n=1
and where the left hand side is replaced by
the cohomology of a discretely ringed adic space.

12.
Comparison with the tame site
(X/S)_{t}
from
[
HS20
]

Let
X\to S
be a morphism of schemes.

12.1
.

Recall that the tame site
(X/S)_{t}
from
[
HS20
, Definition 2.3]
has the category of étale
X
-schemes
as underlying category and
a covering
\{U_{i}\to U\}_{i\in I}
is an étale covering, such that for every
(x,v,\varepsilon_{v})\in\mathrm{Spa}(U,S)
,
there exists
i\in I
and
(y,w,\varepsilon_{w})\in\mathrm{Spa}(U_{i},S)
such that

(k(y),w)/(k(x),v)
is tame.

Proposition 12.2
.

Let
\tilde{X}\to S
a proper morphism and
X\hookrightarrow\tilde{X}
be a quasi-compact open immersion of qcqs schemes.
Then there are adjoint functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X,\tilde{X})_{t})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X/S)_{t}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}}

with
u^{*}
exact, such that for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X,\tilde{X})_{t})
and
U\in(X/S)_{t}
affine,
u_{*}F(U)=F(U,\tilde{U})
for any choice of a Nagata compactification
U\hookrightarrow\tilde{U}
of
U\to\tilde{X}
, and for
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X/S)_{t})
,
u^{*}G
is the sheafification of the presheaf
(U,\tilde{U})\mapsto G(U)
. If
(U,\tilde{U})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
with
\tilde{U}\to\tilde{X}
separated and
universally closed, then
u^{*}G(U,\tilde{U})=G(U)
, in particular
G\cong u_{*}u^{*}G
.

Proof.

Consider the subsite
(X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc}\subseteq(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
consisting of
(U,\tilde{U})
such that
\tilde{U}\to\tilde{X}
is separated and universally closed. Consider the functor:

\Upsilon\colon(X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc}\to(X/S)_{t},\quad(U,\tilde{U})\mapsto U.

Then
[
Sta16
,
Tag 03A0
]
gives an equivalence of topoi

\Upsilon^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc})\cong\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X/S)_{t})\colon\Upsilon_{*}.

Indeed:

(1)

\Upsilon
is cocontinuous. Let
(U,\tilde{U})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc}
and let
\{f_{i}\colon V_{i}\to U\}
be a covering.
Up to refining we can assume that all
V_{i}
are affine, therefore
V_{i}\to\tilde{U}
are separated and of finite type.
Thus we can choose a Nagata compactification
\tilde{V}_{i}
giving a covering
\{(V_{i},\tilde{V}_{i})\to(U,\tilde{U})\}
in
(X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc}
.

(2)

\Upsilon
is continuous. If
\{(V_{i},\tilde{V}_{i})\to(U,\tilde{U})\}
is a tame covering in
(X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc}
, then by the valuative criterion for universal closedness
[
Sta16
,
Tag 01KA
]
,
\{V_{i}\to U\}
is a covering in
(X/S)_{t}
, and
\Upsilon
respects fiber products by definition.

(3)

Given maps in
(X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc}

(f,\tilde{f}_{1}),(f,\tilde{f}_{2})\colon(U^{\prime},\tilde{U}^{\prime})\to(U,\tilde{U}),

consider the closure
\overline{U^{\prime}}
of
U^{\prime}
in
\tilde{U}^{\prime}
. By Remark
2.5
,
(id,\iota)\colon(U^{\prime},\overline{U^{\prime}})\to(U^{\prime},\tilde{U}^{\prime})
is a covering in
(X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc}
and
U^{\prime}
is an open dense in
\overline{U^{\prime}}
. Since
\tilde{U}\to S
is separated by assumption, the equalizer of
\tilde{f}_{1}\iota
and
\tilde{f}_{2}\iota
is closed in
\overline{U^{\prime}}
by
[
Sta16
,
Tag 01KM
]
, and since it contains
U^{\prime}
we conclude that
\tilde{f}_{1}\iota=\tilde{f}_{2}\iota
.

(4)

Given
(U,\tilde{U}),(V,\tilde{V})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc}
and
c\colon U\to V
étale, we can cover
U
by affine schemes
U_{i}
, so that
U_{i}\to\tilde{U}
and
U_{i}\to\tilde{V}
are separated étale maps between qcqs schemes (hence again of finite type). Let
U_{i,1}
and
U_{i,2}
be Nagata compactifications of
U_{i}\to\tilde{U}
and
U_{i}\to\tilde{V}
, respectively. Since the compositions
U_{i}\to U_{i,1}\to\tilde{X}
and
U_{i}\to U_{i,2}\to\tilde{X}
agree, this gives a map
\varphi_{12}\colon U_{i}\to U_{i,1}\times_{\tilde{X}}U_{i,2}
.
The map
\varphi_{12}
is quasi-finite. Indeed, it is separated of finite type since both
U_{i}\to U_{i,1}
and
U_{i}\to U_{i,2}
are quasi-compact open immersions, and it has isolated fibers. Then by Zariski’s Main Theorem
[
Sta16
,
Tag 05K0
]
the map
\varphi_{12}
factors as a quasi-compact open immersion
U_{i}\to\tilde{U}_{i}
followed by a finite morphism
\tilde{U}_{i}\to U_{i,1}\times_{\tilde{X}}U_{i,2}
, therefore
\tilde{U}_{i}\to\tilde{X}
is separated and universally closed. This gives a covering
\{(f_{i},\tilde{f}_{i})\colon(U_{i},\tilde{U}_{i})\to(U,\tilde{U})\}
together with a map
(c_{i},\tilde{c}_{i})\colon(U_{i},\tilde{U}_{i})\to(V,\tilde{V})
such that
c_{i}=c\circ f_{i}
.

(5)

Given
V\in(X/S)_{t}
, we can cover
V
by affine schemes
V_{i}
, so that
V_{i}\to\tilde{X}
is a separated étale map between qcqs schemes.
By taking a Nagata compactification
\tilde{V}_{i}
of
V_{i}\to\tilde{X}

we obtain a covering
\{V_{i}=\Upsilon(V_{i},\tilde{V}_{i})\to V\}
in
(X/S)_{t}
.

In particular, for every
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X/S)_{t})
and
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc})
, there are unique sheaves

\widetilde{G}:=\Upsilon_{*}G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc})
and
\widetilde{F}:=\Upsilon^{*}F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X/S)_{t})
such that
\widetilde{G}(U,\tilde{U})=G(U)
,
for
(U,\tilde{U})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc}
, and
\widetilde{F}(U)=F(U,\tilde{U})
, for
U\in(X/S)_{t}
affine and any Nagata compactification
\tilde{U}
of
U\to\tilde{X}
. Consider the inclusion
u^{\prime}\colon(X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc}\hookrightarrow(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
, which is continuous by definition.
We obtain the adjoint functors

(u^{\prime})^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc})\leftrightarrows\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X,\tilde{X}))\colon(u^{\prime})_{*}.

The functors in the statement are defined as
u_{*}:=\Upsilon^{*}(u^{\prime})_{*}
and
u^{*}:=(u^{\prime})^{*}\Upsilon_{*}
. Since
\Upsilon^{*}
is both left and right adjoint of
\Upsilon_{*}
, we obtain immediately that
u^{*}
is left adjoint to
u_{*}

We compute them. Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X,\tilde{X})_{t})
and let
U\in(X/S)_{t}
be affine, then by definition
u_{*}F(U)=((u^{\prime})_{*}F)(U,\tilde{U})=F(U,\tilde{U})
for any choice of a Nagata compactification
\tilde{U}
of
U\to X\to\tilde{X}
, and for
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X/S)_{t})
,
u^{*}G=(u^{\prime})^{*}\Upsilon_{*}G
is the sheafification of the presheaf
(u^{\prime})^{p}\widetilde{G}
on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
defined by the rule

(12.2.1)

(u^{\prime})^{p}\widetilde{G}\colon(U,\tilde{U})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t}\mapsto\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(U,\tilde{U})\to(V,\tilde{V})\\
\tilde{V}\to\tilde{X}\textrm{sep. univ. closed}\end{subarray}}G(V).

Let
(V,\tilde{V})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc}
and let
(g,\tilde{g}):(U,\tilde{U})\to(V,\tilde{V})
be a morphism in
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
.

Claim 12.3
.

There is an affine open covering
\tilde{U}=\cup_{i}\tilde{U}_{i}
and quasi-compact open immersions
\tilde{U}_{i}\to\overline{U_{i}}

such that
(U_{i}:=U\cap\tilde{U}_{i},\overline{U_{i}})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc}
and the composition

(U_{i},\tilde{U}_{i})\to(U,\tilde{U})\to(V,\tilde{V})
factors via a morphism
(U_{i},\overline{U_{i}})\to(V,\tilde{V})
in
(X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc}
, for all
i
.

Indeed, the statement is Zariski local on
\tilde{X}
and as
\tilde{U}\xrightarrow{\tilde{g}}\tilde{V}
is ift,
we may
assume that
\tilde{U}
is affine and
\tilde{g}:\tilde{U}\to\tilde{V}
factors as

\tilde{U}\xrightarrow{\tilde{g}_{1}}\tilde{V}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\tilde{g}_{2}}\tilde{V}
with
\tilde{V}^{\prime}
affine,
\tilde{g}_{1}
integral, and
\tilde{g}_{2}
of finite type.
Considering a Nagata compactification
\overline{\tilde{W}}\to\tilde{V}
of this latter morphism,
we see that
\tilde{U}\to\tilde{V}
factors as a composition

\tilde{U}\xrightarrow{h}\tilde{W}\xrightarrow{j}\overline{\tilde{W}}\xrightarrow{f}\tilde{V},

with
h
an integral morphism,
j
a quasi-compact open immersion, and
f
a proper morphism.
By Lemma
1.6
, we find
\overline{\tilde{U}}
, a quasi-compact open immersion
U\to\tilde{U}\to\overline{\tilde{U}}
and

\overline{\tilde{U}}\to\overline{\tilde{W}}\to\tilde{V}
a composition of an integral and a proper morphism, so separated and universally closed. Therefore
(U,\overline{\tilde{U}})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t,suc}
and this proves the claim.

We conclude as follows. Consider the presheaf

G^{\prime}\colon(U,\tilde{U})\in(X,\tilde{X})_{t}\mapsto G(U).

By construction, there exists a map
(u^{\prime})^{p}\widetilde{G}\to G^{\prime}
.
By what we observed before, for all
(U,\tilde{U})\to(V,\tilde{V})
with
\tilde{V}\to\tilde{X}
separated and universally closed there exists a covering
(U_{i},\tilde{U}_{i})
of
(U,\tilde{U})
such that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(u^{\prime})^{p}\widetilde{G}(U,\tilde{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime}(U,\tilde{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(U_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(u^{\prime})^{p}\widetilde{G}(U_{i},\tilde{U}_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime}(U_{i},\tilde{U}_{i}).}

Let
a\in(u^{\prime})^{p}\widetilde{G}(U,\tilde{U})
and let
(U,\tilde{U})\to(V,\tilde{V})
as in (
12.2.1
) such that there exists
b\in G(V)
that maps to
a
, and let
\{(U_{i},\tilde{U}_{i})\}
be a covering such that a diagram as above exists. Assume that
a\mapsto 0
in
G^{\prime}(U,\tilde{U})
, then we conclude that
b_{|U_{i}}=0
, so
a_{|(U_{i},\tilde{U}_{i})}=0
, which implies that the map
(u^{\prime})^{p}\widetilde{G}\to G^{\prime}
is injective after sheafification. Let now
b\in G(U)=G^{\prime}(U,\tilde{U})
. By applying the above diagram with
(V,\tilde{V})=(X,\tilde{X})
, there exists a covering
\{(U_{i},\tilde{U}_{i})\}
such that
b_{U_{i}}
is in the image of
(u^{\prime})^{p}\widetilde{G}(U_{i},\tilde{U}_{i})\to G^{\prime}(U_{i},\tilde{U}_{i})
. Hence the map
(u^{\prime})^{p}\widetilde{G}\to G^{\prime}
is surjective after sheafification.
Moreover, if
\tilde{U}\to\tilde{X}
is separated and universally closed, then
G^{\prime}
satisfies descent on
(U,\tilde{U})
. Indeed,
if
\{(V_{i},\tilde{V}_{i})\to(U,\tilde{U})\}
is a covering in
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
, then
\{V_{i}\to U\}

is a tame covering in
(X/S)_{t}
, as we have bijections
\mathrm{Spa}(U,\tilde{U})\leftrightarrow\mathrm{Spa}(U,\tilde{X})\leftrightarrow\mathrm{Spa}(U,S)
given by the valuative criterion for separatedness and universal closedness
[
Sta16
,
Tag 01KA
and
Tag 01KY
]
. Therefore in this case
we have
u^{*}G(U,\tilde{U})=G^{\prime}(U,\tilde{U})=G(U)
.
Finally, for
U\in(X/S)_{t}
affine, the map
U\to\tilde{X}
is separated of finite type so it always admits a Nagata compactification
\tilde{U}
, and we have
u_{*}u^{*}G(U)=u^{*}G(U,\tilde{U})=G(U)
.
This implies
G\cong u_{*}u^{*}G
.
∎

Remark 12.4
.

(1)

Notice that for
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X/S)_{t})
the presheaf
(U,\tilde{U})\mapsto G(U)
on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
does not necessarily have descent if
\tilde{U}\to\tilde{X}
is not proper and
G
is not an étale sheaf on
X
. Indeed, let
S
be a scheme with
p\mathcal{O}_{S}=0
and consider
(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S},\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S})
in
(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S},\mathbf{P}^{1}_{S})_{t}
. Then, writing
\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}=\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{S}[x]
, the Artin–Schreier cover
C\to\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}
defined by
t^{p}-t=x
gives a covering
(C,C)\to(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S},\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S})
in
(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S},\mathbf{P}^{1}_{S})_{t}
, but it is not a tame covering in
(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}/S)_{t}
.

(2)

Consider the sheaf
\mathcal{O}^{t}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X,\tilde{X})_{t})
from Lemma
8.5
.
Then,

u_{*}\mathcal{O}^{t}(U)=\mathcal{O}^{t}(U,\overline{U})=\mathcal{O}(\overline{U})=\mathcal{O}(T)\text{ for }U\in X_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}},

where
U\hookrightarrow\overline{U}
is the integral closure in
U
of a Nagata compactification of
U\to\tilde{X}
, and

\overline{U}\to T\to\tilde{X}
is the Stein factorization of the universally closed morphism
\overline{U}\to\tilde{X}
.

(3)

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X,\tilde{X})_{t})
, the map
u^{*}u_{*}F\to F
is not an isomorphism in general. If that was the case, then for
(U,\tilde{U})=\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}(U_{\lambda},\tilde{U}_{\lambda})\in\widetilde{(X,\tilde{X})_{t}}
tamely acyclic, the above computation gives that

u^{*}u_{*}\mathcal{O}^{t}(U,\tilde{U})=u^{p}u_{*}\mathcal{O}^{t}(U,\tilde{U})=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{\lambda}\mathcal{O}(T_{\lambda})

where
T_{\lambda}\to\tilde{X}
is integral coming from the Stein factorization of
\overline{U_{\lambda}}
as above. Therefore, this would imply that the map
\mathcal{O}(\tilde{X})\to\mathcal{O}(\tilde{U})
is integral, which does not hold in general.
This implies that there is no object
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X/S)_{t})
such that
u^{*}G=\mathcal{O}^{t}
.
Else we would have by Proposition
12.2
that
G=u_{*}u^{*}G=u_{*}\mathcal{O}^{t}
, hence

\mathcal{O}^{t}=u^{*}G=u^{*}u_{*}\mathcal{O}^{t}
.

Theorem 12.5
.

Let
S
be an affine scheme, let
\tilde{X}\to S
be a proper morphism and
X\hookrightarrow\tilde{X}
be a quasi-compact open immersion.
Consider the adjoint functors
u^{*}\dashv u_{*}
of Proposition
12.2
. Then for all
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X/S)_{t})
we have

R\Gamma((X/S)_{t},G)\simeq R\Gamma((X,\tilde{X})_{t},u^{*}G).

Proof.

We need to show that
R^{q}u_{*}u^{*}G=0
for
q>0
. Indeed, if this holds, we have that the map
G\to Ru_{*}u^{*}G
is an equivalence, therefore

R\Gamma((X/S)_{t},G)\simeq R\Gamma((X/S)_{t},Ru_{*}u^{*}G)\simeq R\Gamma((X,\tilde{X})_{t},u^{*}G).

Let
(\overline{x},\overline{v})
be a tame point of
X
in the sense of
[
HS20
, Definition 2.7]
, then

(R^{q}u_{*}u^{*}G)_{(\overline{x},\overline{v})}=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{U\to X}H^{q}((U,\tilde{U})_{t},u^{*}G),

where the colimit on the right hand side is indexed along all étale neighborhoods
U\to X
of
\overline{x}
which are tame at
(\overline{x},\overline{v})
, and
\tilde{U}\to\tilde{X}
a choice of a Nagata compactification of
U\to\tilde{X}
.
We can always suppose that
U
is affine, therefore the map
U\to S
is quasi-projective. By Chow’s lemma and the control of the birational locus of Raynaud and Gruson (see
[
Con07
, Corollary 2.6]
), we can choose
\tilde{U}
quasi-projective over
S
.
Hence
\tilde{U}
satisfies the property that every finite set of points is contained in an affine open. Thus
by Theorem
6.5

\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{U\to X}H^{q}((U,\tilde{U})_{t},u^{*}G)\cong\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{U\to X}\check{H}^{q}((U,\tilde{U})_{t},u^{*}G),

Recall that
u^{*}G
is the sheafification of the presheaf
(V,\tilde{V})\mapsto G(V)
. Since Čech cohomology of presheaves is invariant under taking sheafification by Theorem
6.5
, we have that

\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{U\to X}\check{H}^{q}((U,\tilde{U})_{t},u^{*}G)\cong\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{(V,\tilde{V})\to(U,\tilde{U})}\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{U\to X}\pi_{-q}G(V^{\times\bullet}),

where we can suppose that
(V,\tilde{V})\to(U,\tilde{U})
ranges over all coverings in
(X,\tilde{X})_{{\rm affine},t}
by Lemma
2.9
, and moreover by Claim
12.3
that factors through
(V,\overline{V})\to(U,\tilde{U})
in
(X,\tilde{X})_{\rm suc}
.
Since
\pi_{-q}G(V^{\times\bullet})
does not depend on
\tilde{V}
, we can in fact suppose that
\tilde{V}=\overline{V}
, therefore we have that

\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{U\to X}\check{H}^{q}((U,\tilde{U})_{t},u^{*}G)\cong\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{(V,\overline{V})\to(U,\tilde{U})}\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{U\to X}\pi_{-q}G(V^{\times\bullet}),

where
(V,\tilde{V})\to(U,\tilde{U})
ranges over all coverings in
(X,\tilde{X})_{suc,t}
with
V
affine. By the valuative criterion (as in the proof of Proposition
12.2
) this implies that
V\to U
is a covering in
(X/S)_{t}
, and every covering
V\to U
in
(X/S)_{t}
with
V
affine induces a covering
(V,\overline{V})\to(U,\tilde{U})
in
(X,\tilde{X})_{suc,t}
by the choice of a Nagata compactification, therefore

\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{(V,\overline{V})\to(U,\tilde{U})}\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{U\to X}\pi_{-q}G(V^{\times\bullet})\cong\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{V\to U}\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{U\to X}\pi_{-q}G(V^{\times\bullet}),

where on the right
\{V\to U\}
is the cofiltered set of coverings in
(X/S)_{t}
and
\{U\to X\}
is the cofiltered set of tame neighborhoods of
(\overline{x},\overline{v})
, therefore by
[
HS20
, Theorem 7.16]
it is

\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{U\to X}\check{H}^{q}((U/S)_{t},G)=H^{q}((\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{X,(\overline{x},\overline{v})}^{t})/S)_{t},G)=0.

∎

Remark 12.6
.

We observe that for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X,\tilde{X})_{t})
, the cohomology groups

H^{i}((X,\tilde{X})_{t},F)\quad\text{and}\quad H^{i}((X/S)_{t},u_{*}F)=H^{i}((X,\tilde{X})_{t},u^{*}u_{*}F))

are in general not equal, even if
\tilde{X}\to S
is proper and
F
is constructed as in (
8.1
).
Indeed, assume
S=\operatorname{Spec}(k)
with
k
an algebraically closed field of characteristic
p>0

and assume
\dim X\leq 3
. Take
F=\mathcal{O}^{t}
.
We get from Remark
12.4
that
u_{*}\mathcal{O}^{t}
agrees with the constant sheaf
k
on
(X/S)_{t}
, so we have by
Theorem
7.2

H^{i}((X/S)_{t},u_{*}\mathcal{O}^{t})=\varinjlim_{\tilde{Y}}H^{i}(\tilde{Y}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}},j_{*}^{(Y,\tilde{Y})}u^{*}k),

where the direct limit is over all smooth compactifications of
X
. By construction,
j_{*}^{(Y,\tilde{Y})}u^{*}k
agrees with the constant étale sheaf
k
on
\tilde{Y}
.
Furthermore we have

(12.6.1)

H^{i}(\tilde{Y}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}},k)=H^{i}(\tilde{Y}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}},\mathbb{Z}/p)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}k.

For this we observe, that for any étale sheaf
G
on
\tilde{Y}
, the presheaf
U\mapsto G(U)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}k
is already a sheaf,
since we can identify its sections as an abelian group with a direct sum over a basis of
k/\mathbb{F}_{p}
,
which is a sheaf as the site
\tilde{Y}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}

is noetherian. It also follows that the functor
-\otimes k:\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}(\tilde{Y}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}},\mathbb{F}_{p})\to\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}(\tilde{Y}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}},k)
is exact and maps

\Gamma
-acyclic sheaves to
\Gamma
-acyclic sheaves. This gives (
12.6.1
).
Furthermore, we remark that
Z\mapsto H^{i}(Z_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}},\mathbb{Z}/p)
is a birational invariant on smooth proper schemes.
Indeed this follows from the fact that proper correspondences act on

H^{i}(Z_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}},\mathbb{Z}/p)=H^{i}(Z_{\rm Zar},[\mathcal{O}_{Z}\xrightarrow{{\rm Frob}-{\rm id}}\mathcal{O}_{Z}]),

by
[
BRS22
, 9.11]
and the fact that the action of the closure of a graph of a birational
(maybe not everywhere defined) map
Z\dashrightarrow Z^{\prime}
induces an isomorphism
H^{i}(Z,\mathcal{O}_{Z})\xrightarrow{\simeq}H^{i}(Z^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\prime}})
,
by
[
CR11
, Theorem 1]
. Thus altogether we find

(12.6.2)

H^{i}((X/S)_{t},u_{*}\mathcal{O}^{t})=H^{i}(\tilde{X}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}},\mathbb{Z}/p)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}k,

where
\tilde{X}
is some smooth compactification of
X
.
On the other hand by Remark
8.7
and the birational invariance of
\mathcal{O}
-cohomology on smooth proper schemes we have

(12.6.3)

H^{i}((X,\tilde{X})_{t},\mathcal{O}^{t})=H^{i}(\tilde{X},\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{X}}),

The groups (
12.6.2
) and (
12.6.3
) are equal for all
i
if and only if the Frobenius
acts bijectively on
H^{i}(\tilde{X},\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{X}})
. This is, e.g., not the case if
\tilde{X}
is a supersingular abelian variety.

We conclude the section with a computation of the cohomology of
u_{*}F_{\beta^{\rm rec}_{\log}}
for
F
a reciprocity sheaf,
see (
11.1
) for notation.

Lemma 12.7
.

Let
k
,
X\subset\tilde{X}
,
D
, and
F
be as in Lemma
11.2
above.
Assume additionally that
\tilde{X}
has dimension
d
and that

(
res
)

d

holds (e.g.
d\leq 3
).
Denote by
h^{0}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)
the maximal
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-invariant subsheaf with transfers of
F
, which is a Nisnevich sheaf
(e.g.
[
RS21
, 4.30]
), and by
h^{0,t}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)
the presheaf on
(X/k)_{t}
given by

V\mapsto h^{0}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)(V)
. Then

u_{*}(F_{\beta^{\rm rec}_{\log}})=h^{0,t}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F),

where
u_{*}:\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X,\tilde{X})_{t})\to\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}((X/k)_{t})
is the functor from Proposition
12.2
.
In particular,
h^{0,t}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)
is a sheaf on
(X/k)_{t}
and if
\tilde{X}
is additionally proper, we get

R\Gamma((X/k)_{t},u_{*}(F_{\beta^{\rm rec}_{\log}}))=R\Gamma((X/k)_{t},h^{0,t}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F))=R\Gamma((X,\tilde{X})_{t},u^{*}h^{0,t}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)).

Proof.

The second statement follows directly from the first and Theorem
12.5
.
As
h^{0}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)
is a Zariski sheaf it suffices to show the equality of the first statement when evaluated on
an affine étale
X
-scheme
V
.
By

(
res
)

d

we find a smooth compactification
\overline{V}
of
V
with
simple normal crossing complement
D_{V}=(\overline{V}\setminus V)_{{\operatorname{red}}}
.
By Proposition
12.2
together with Lemma
11.2
we have

(12.7.1)

u_{*}(F_{\beta^{\rm rec}_{\log}})(V)=F_{\beta^{\rm rec}_{\log}}(V,\overline{V})={\underline{\omega}}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\overline{V},D_{V}).

By
[
Sai21
, Theorem 4.2]
for each map
\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{v}^{h}\to\overline{V}
,
with
\mathcal{O}^{h}_{v}
a henselian discrete valuation ring of geometric type over
k
,
we get an induced morphism

\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\overline{V},D_{V})\to\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\mathcal{O}_{v}^{h},\mathfrak{m}_{v}^{-1})
.
Therefore, the right hand side of (
12.7.1
) is precisely the subset of
F(V)
consisting of
the sections with all the motivic conductors
\leq 1
. Thus by
[
RS21
, Corollary 4.32]
,

(12.7.2)

{\underline{\omega}}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F(\overline{V},D_{V})=h^{0}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)(V),

which yields the statement.
∎

Remark 12.8
.

Let
X
,
\tilde{X}
and
F
be as above.
If
h^{0}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)
happens to be an étale sheaf on
X
, then the presheaf

(V,\tilde{V})\mapsto h^{0}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)(V)
is also a sheaf on
(X,\tilde{X})_{t}
and is equal to
u^{*}h^{0,t}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)
,
by Proposition
12.2
.

Note however that
h^{0}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)
will in positive characteristic
p
in general not be an étale sheaf even if
F
is.
For example consider
F=\Omega^{q}
and assume

(
res
)

d

, with
d=\dim X
.
By
[
RS21
, (4) on p. 5]
and (
12.7.2
) the Nisnevich sheaf
h^{0}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F)
on
X
is given by

V\mapsto H^{0}(\overline{V},\Omega^{q}_{\overline{V}/k}(\log D)),

where
(\overline{V},D)
is a normal crossing compactification of
V
.
It is well known that this is not an étale sheaf. Indeed,
consider any form
\alpha\in\Omega^{1}_{k(x,y)}
, which has not only log-poles, e.g.
\alpha=dx/y
.
By
[
Mum61
, I, Theorem]
and resolution of singularities in dimension 2,
there is a dense open
V\subset\mathbf{P}^{2}
and a projective morphism
f:X\to\mathbf{P}^{2}
from a smooth
k
-scheme
X
,
such that
\alpha\in H^{0}(V,\Omega^{1}_{V})
, the induced map
f^{-1}(V)\to V
is finite étale,

D=f^{-1}(\mathbf{P}^{2}\setminus V)_{{\operatorname{red}}}
is an SNCD, and
f^{*}\alpha\in H^{0}(X,\Omega^{1}_{X})\subset H^{0}(X,\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log D))
.
In this case clearly the pullbacks of
f^{*}\alpha
to any normal crossing model of

f^{-1}(U)\times_{U}f^{-1}(U)
along the maps induced by the two projections agree, but it does not descent to a logarithmic form on a normal crossing model of
U
by our choice of
\alpha
.
